 JOWIH FOUNDATION - DRAFT CONSTITUTION

Preamble 

We, the Founder and the undersigned, in recognition of the inherent dignity, equality, and developmental rights of all persons within the African continent and diaspora; acknowledging the persistent structural barriers that impede social mobility, technological capability, and sustainable economic advancement; and affirming the necessity of institutions that endure beyond individual leadership or political cycles-hereby establish the Jowih Foundation as a permanent, mission-driven development organization to Accelerate development across the continent through THE GREAT AFRICAN GRID initiative. The Great African Grid is not a political union. It is not a supranational authority. It does not replace or subordinate any national government, central bank, or legal system.It is an infrastructure and economic coordination framework - designed to enable sovereign nations to cooperate more efficiently, trade more freely, and develop more rapidly, without surrendering control over their own affairs.

Africa’s opportunity lies in harnessing collective vision and executing at scale. To complement and enhance existing efforts, the Jowih Foundation focuses on Accelerating National and Continental progress in areas where technology, sustained capital, and long-term collaboration can amplify impact. We empower governments,citizens, talent, partnerships, and mission-locked resources to design, fund, and operate transformative social and technological interventions that strengthen human capital development, accelerate digital and industrial competitiveness, and ensure that the African continent is an active and sovereign participant in the global innovation economy. Its mandate encompasses humanitarian upliftment, youth empowerment, strategic research investment, and the advancement of Africa-centered technologies and infrastructure.

The Foundation serves as a Development Accelerator through its initiative called The Great African Grid(THE GAG) in most areas where African governments are stracturally or financially limited . Vision Horizon 2045 is not the end. It is the first milestone.

It operates in full compliance with sovereign laws, in cooperative partnership with governments, and in alignment with National priorities where invited. We uphold independent governance, an immutable mission-lock, and rigorous protections for user and beneficiary data to ensure trust and sustainability.

The Foundation’s primary funding engine is the reinvestment of commercial returns: eighty percent (90%) of net profits from the Jowih Holding Company shall be devoted to the Mission Fund and reinvested in continental development. Voluntary small contributions, and concessional support are complementary and shall not replace the primary reinvestment model.

As the Foundation advances implementation across its four strategic pillars-(i) Empower Communities & Basic Needs; (ii) Digital Opportunity & Youth Advancement; (iii) African Research & Innovation Leadership; and (iv) The Future Civilization (the Cyberpunk Africa vision)-and as citizen-owned capital and commercial reinvestment expand economic activity and create shared value, participating governments and communities will be better positioned to pursue mutual priorities. This collaborative dynamic is central to achieving the first milestone of The GAG’s 20-Year Pan-African Transformation Strategy The 2045 Horizon and to realizing a prosperous, technologically-advanced Futuristic Africa owned by its people.

This Constitution is adopted as the supreme governing instrument of the Foundation, safeguarding mission integrity, fiduciary accountability, operational independence, and continuity of purpose. All Foundation activities, including affiliated Operating Companies, shall be executed in alignment with this Constitution, applicable laws, and principles of good governance, transparency, and ethical stewardship.

The Foundation is established as a Company Limited by Guarantee under the laws of Mauritius (or any successor jurisdiction as approved) and shall operate in compliance with applicable law in jurisdictions where it works. We commit to transparency, auditable stewardship of funds, strict anti-diversion controls, child protection, and the highest standards of data privacy and security.The Foundation shall abide by all applicable laws and shall strictly oppose any illegal measures directed against it, its assets, or its personnel.

Through this Constitution, the Founder and signatories commit to the long-term transformation of African societies by fostering resilient communities, locally owned innovation, and equitable access to the opportunities of the twenty-first and twenty-second century economy: citizen-owned assets, accountable governance, and sustained execution to enable an empowered, connected, and prosperous Africa.

ARTICLE I - THE GAG, DEFINITIONS & INTERPRETATION

1.1 Definitions  

In this Constitution, unless the context otherwise requires:  

. “THE GAG” The Great African Grid is the Foundation's continental infrastructure and economic development strategy.It is not one project. It is the sum of everything - physical infrastructure, digital systems, industrial capacity, human development, and financial rails that connect Africa to itself and to the future.

The GAG is the backbone of First World Africa. It is the operating system for a continent that has been waiting for a workable vision.

· "Articles" means the Articles of this Constitution. 

. “Private constitution ANNEXES” means a separate confidential internal constitution working in harmony with this constitution to achieve a common goal 

. “Governance OS” means a mesh or interconnected blockchain high tech system used to manage governance and strategies within the foundation, its subsidiaries, trusts and companies with high end security and immutable records.

· "Independent Auditor General" means the Independent Auditor General established under Article XLIII.  

· " Foundation Board" means the Board of Governors of the Foundation.  

. “Combined Trust Board” means a board composed of Purpose Trust and Family Trust board members.

· "CEO" means the Chief Executive Officer.  

· "Community Development Enterprise" or "CDE" means a community-owned enterprise established under Article LIII.2.1(c) in each rural region, comprising five specialized entities (Education & Scholarship, Agriculture & Food Security, Health & Wellness, Infrastructure & Energy, Digital & Innovation) that combine community development with sustainable business operations. CDEs are not lending institutions; they do not issue loans or charge interest. They are development vehicles that reinvest profits into community projects. Seventy percent (70%) of CDE funds are locked for community development projects; thirty percent (30%) are for community-owned businesses; thirty percent (30%) of net business profits flow to the Jowih Care Fund.

· "External Audit Firm" means the independent external auditor appointed.  

· "Steward President & Founder" means Reuben Oduk (designated Life Visionary Founder).  

-"Foundation" or "Jowih Foundation" means the Company Limited by Guarantee established under this Constitution, serving as the mission-funding and asset-holding layer that receives 90% economic benefits from the Jowih Holding Company and applies them for social impact. It is the supreme governing entity under this Constitution, to which all other entities (Trusts, Operating Companies, ADN) are subordinate in matters of mission and fiduciary duty.

· "Mission Fund" means the designated fund/repository for social mission expenditures.  

· "Referendum Commission" means the independent commission established under Article XLIV to oversee public referendums within the foundation.  

· "Jowih COS" means the commercial digital infrastructure (Jowih) 

· "Member(s)" means the persons admitted as members under Article IV.  

· "Beneficiary" means persons or groups the Foundation is established to benefit.  

· "Core Articles" means Articles expressly designated as core and subject to the highest amendment threshold.  

· "External Auditor" means a certified external auditor firm not related to the Foundation or its officers.  

· "Jowih Purpose Trust" means the irrevocable trust established to hold 60% voting shares in the Jowih Holding Company, enforce the Mission,  with trustees acting as fiduciaries without personal profit. Togethor with Family Trust they ensure thst the Foundation receives it’s 90% economic benefit.

· "Founder Family Trust" means the trust holding the Founder's 10% economic shares and 40% voting shares in the Jowih Holding for mission continuity & family security, bound by ethical and anti-diversion rules.  

· "Operating Companies" means trading or subsidiary entities (e.g., Jowih Technologies Ltd or The Jowih CRT company) generating profits for the Mission. 

. “Holding Company” means the company that owans and manages all future companies and subsidiaries under the foundations including the Jowih IP company

· "Other Mission Companies" means any operating company other than Jowih Technologies Company.  

· "Strategic Acquisition" means the purchase of existing commercial enterprises by the Foundation under Article XLV.  

· "Acquisition Review Panel" means the special committee established under Article XLV to evaluate proposed acquisitions.  

· "Mission Alignment Period" means the 180-day period following acquisition during which a company must adopt the Foundation's governance structure. 

-"Technology Ethics Council" means the part-time, ad-hoc expert body established under Article XXXIX.3, which provides strategic guidance and ethical certification for technology initiatives.

-"Continental Development Assembly (CDA)" means the council composed of PAN-Ambassadors & Diaspora Ambassador meeting in assembly, as defined in Article LIV.2. The CDA is not a separate legal entity from the PAN-Ambassadors.

-"Community Participation Framework" or “Community Development Framework” means the integrated system for community and beneficiary input, which synergistically utilizes Beneficiary Advisory Councils (BACs), Community Steering Assemblies (CSAs), Citizen Assemblies, and the Comrade Hub, as detailed in Article XXIII.2.

· "Family Protocol" means the separate binding document established by the Founder for Life Visionary nomination and succession under Article VII.  

· "Steward President & Life Visionary" means the successor role to the Founder for safeguarding moral and strategic intent under Article VII.  

· "Vision Advisory Notice" means the written notice issued by the Founder or Life Visionary to suspend a Board decision under Article VII.  

· "Asset Lock" means the permanent dedication of all Foundation assets to mission use, prohibiting private gain, as detailed in Article XXV.  

· "Assets" means all financial, physical, intellectual property, equity, and future assets of the Foundation as defined in Article XXV.  

· "Mission Investor Shares" means non-voting, redeemable shares issued for mission-aligned capital under Article XXXIII.  

· "Oversight Panel" means the independent panel of experts for reviewing amendments, conflicts, and investigations under Article XIX and related articles.

. “Trustees Oversight Committee” means an independent panel of experts for reviewing conflicts and investigations concerning trustees.  

· "Public Dashboard" means the publicly accessible digital platform for real-time transparency under Article XXXV.  

· "Ghost Worker" means any individual receiving compensation without performing bona fide services, as defined in Article XI.  

· "FTE" means full-time equivalent employee count under Article XI.  

· "Beneficiary Advisory Councils" or "BACs" means councils of beneficiaries for program input under Article XXIII.  

· "Community Steering Assemblies" or "CSAs" means regional assemblies for community consultation under Article XXIII.  

· "Comrade Hub" means the integrated ecosystem of general citizens, university students, academic institutions, entrepreneurial networks, and community volunteers across all African jurisdictions, working collectively to support the Foundation's mission through innovation, talent development, and scalable implementation under Article XXIII.

· "Political Activity" means partisan actions such as endorsements or campaign contributions, prohibited under Article XLVII.  

· "Emergency Council" means the body activated for Level 3 emergencies under Article XLVI.  

· "Emergency Levels" means the classification of crises (Minor, Major, Existential) under Article XLVI.  

· "FPIC" means Free, Prior, and Informed Consent for community projects under Article XXXVIII.  

· "Data Integrity Ledger" means the cryptographically-secured system for recording disbursements and transactions as defined in Article V.A.4.

· "Enhanced Oversight Protocol" means the special monitoring procedures activated for cash exceptions under Article V.A.1.

· "Mission-Threatening Action" means any decision or activity that could alter, dilute, or deviate from the Foundation's core Mission as defined in Articles II and XLVIII.

· "Trusted Local Partner" means community organizations authorized to provide beneficiary verification under Article V.A.2.

· "Unified Beneficiary Registry" means the centralized database for preventing duplication and fraud in beneficiary identification under Article V.A.2.

· "Contributor Trust Agreement" means the mandatory contract assigning intellectual property to the Foundation as defined in Article LI.3.

· "Mission-Aligned Investment Policy" means the ethical investment framework governing endowment and reserve investments under Article XLI.

· "Golden Vote" means the veto power held by the Founder Family Trust on mission-threatening actions in the foundation and Operating Companies under Article XXXI.7.

· "Mission Alignment Period" means the 180-day restructuring timeframe for acquired companies under Article XLV.3.

· "Tier 1 Core Articles" and "Tier 2 Core Articles" mean the classified constitutional provisions with extraordinary amendment protection as defined in Article XIX.3.

· "Project Cycling" means the prohibited practice of rebranding stalled projects for continued funding as defined in Article XI.7.

. A “Country Budget Pot & Health Pot” is the total capital reserved and allocated by the Foundation’s central treasury for the exclusive purpose of funding all approved missions, operations, and projects within a specific national jurisdiction. It is a virtual, non-discretionary fund managed centrally to ensure financial integrity, with disbursements triggered only upon the verification of pre-agreed milestones.

· "Direct Disbursement Model" means the mandatory system under Article LIII where all funds flow directly from Foundation centralized accounts to verified vendors/beneficiaries, bypassing PAN-Ambassadors and Executive Directors entirely.

“Official Seal” means the embossed or inked metal seal of the Foundation, kept under the joint custody of the Independent Auditor General (IAG) and the Secretary-General. Its use on any document signifies the irrevocable will and authority of the Foundation, binding the Foundation as if executed by its Board of Governors.

· "Zero-Knowledge Infrastructure" means systems where the Foundation cannot access user data even if compelled, implementing end-to-end encryption with user-controlled keys as defined in Article LV.3.

· "Jurisdictional Evasion Protocol" means the infrastructure capability under Article L.2 to rapidly migrate data and services away from jurisdictions attempting unlawful data access.

· "Stipend-Based Compensation" means the modest payment model for Volunteer Leaders covering communication and operational costs, categorized as program implementation expenses rather than staff salaries under Article XI.10.

· "Multi-Jurisdictional Legal Shield" means the protection strategy under Article XXXIX.3 requiring all data access requests to be processed through Mauritian courts under Data Protection Act 2017.

· "Ephemeral Data Principles" means the data handling approach under Article XXXIX.4 featuring default expiration, minimal retention, and cryptographic shredding of outdated information.

· "Data Fragmentation" means the technical safeguard under Article XXXIX distributing user data fragments across multiple legal jurisdictions to prevent complete jurisdictional capture.

.”AD-Hoc” means a temporary/part time and dissolves after achieving the objectives mandated

. “IAG” means the Independent Auditor General 

· "ADN" or "Africa Development Network" means the continental operational presence of the Foundation established under Article LIV, functioning as the principal non-partisan continental coordination body for mission execution and citizen participation across all African jurisdictions.

· "Annual Impact Report" means the comprehensive annual report published by the Foundation under Article XXXV, containing financial statements, audit findings, program impact metrics, and transparency disclosures.

· "Beneficiary Advisory Council" or "BAC" means a council of direct beneficiaries established under Article XXIII to provide program-specific feedback and input on Foundation initiatives.

· "Community Steering Assembly" or "CSA" means a regional assembly established under Article XXIII for broader community consultation on impact, land use, and cultural matters.

· "Country Executive Director" or "CED" means a volunteer leader appointed by a PAN-Ambassador under Article LIV.3 to manage specific operational responsibilities within a country jurisdiction, serving on a stipend basis.

· "Environmental Performance Adjustment" or "EPA" means the dynamic fund reallocation framework established under Article LIII.2.2 that adjusts country budget allocations based on measurable environmental outcomes and citizen participation.

· "Ethics & Safeguarding Committee" means the ad-hoc committee established under Article XXII.4 responsible for investigating ethics violations and safeguarding breaches.

· "Jowih Care Fund" means the dedicated fund established under Article LIII.2.1(e) that receives 30% of net profits from CDEs business ventures to support needy and poor persons within each region.

· "Jowih IP Company" or "Jowih IP Holdings" means the Singapore-registered subsidiary of Jowih Holdings Limited that owns all intellectual property of the foundation assets and operating companies under Article II.4(e) and Article XVI.1.

· "Mission Oath" means the sworn declaration required of all Foundation and trustees members under Article XLVIII.7 to uphold the Foundation's mission as defined in its Constitution.

· "Phased Funding Model" means the dual-track funding model established under Article LIII that combines centralized flagship excellence with decentralized community empowerment across African jurisdictions.

1.2 THE GREAT AFRICAN GRID initiative

SECTION A:WHAT THE GAG IS

The Great African Grid is the Foundation's continental infrastructure and economic development strategy .It is not one project. It is the sum of everything - physical infrastructure, digital systems, industrial capacity, human development, and financial rails that connect Africa to itself and to the future.The GAG shall have always have a  20-year milestone objectives to Accelerate development in Africa for the present and the future generations to come.

The GAG is the backbone of First World Africa. It is the operating system for a continent that has been waiting for a workable vision.

The Great African Grid initiative is built on six founding principles:

a. Extensive Consultation, Joint Contribution, Shared Benefits - All GAG projects are developed in partnership with host governments, local communities, and regional bodies. Every project is a Mission-first to Accelerate Development in Africa. The development strategy is executed through ADFA projects and CDEs projects.

b. Hard Connectivity, Soft Connectivity, Heart Connectivity - Hard connectivity means physical infrastructure not limited to: transport, energy, data cables. Soft connectivity means rules, standards, and policies that enable seamless movement. Heart connectivity means people-to-people ties, cultural exchange, and shared identity.

c. Win-Win Cooperation - Every GAG project delivers value to all parties. Host governments get Near Free Accelerated Development through The GAG without draining the National Treasury with exception of projects under Model A (State Funded).The Other models under ADFA agreement include Model B(Foundation Funded) & Model C (Joint Funded). Communities get millions jobs,skills,improved living standards and services. The Foundation gets returns for the Mission Fund to invest in more project across it’s four pillars.Mission Investors get fair returns. No one is exploited.

d. Africa First, Global Best- The GAG prioritizes African solutions and African ownership though Asset Lock. Where African Private Sector capacity is insufficient, it partners with global leaders - but never becomes dependent on any single country or corporation.

e. INTERGRATE, EMPOWER, COMPLEMENT, ACCELERATE, ENABLE The Great African Grid Initiative does not seek to disrupt, replace, compete, extract, or control.It seeks to intergrate, empower, complement, accelerate, and enable.

SECTION B: THE SIX CORRIDORS

The Great African Grid has seven corridors - each representing a critical infrastructure system. They operate in parallel, not in sequence:

CORRIDOR 1: TRANSPORT CORRIDOR - Moving People and Goods

CORRIDOR 2: INDUSTRIAL & MANUFACTURING CORRIDOR--Building Africa's Production Capacity

CORRIDOR 3: ENERGY CORRIDOR - Powering Everything

CORRIDOR 4: DIGITAL CORRIDOR - Connecting Everyone and Powering the Digital Economy

CORRIDOR 5: PAYMENT CORRIDOR - Moving Value

CORRIDOR 6: SERVICE CORRIDOR - Health, Educating, Empowering

CORRIDOR 7: SPACE CAPABILITY -- Securing African race into the space innovation.

MILESTONES: THE HORIZONS OF THE FUTURE

The Horizon 2045 is the first milestone to achieve The GAG infrastructure and economic strategy lasting 20 years.

The 2045 Horizon can achieved as early Year 10-15, not Year 20. Because the GAG is built in parallel, not in sequence.The next milestone will be Horizon 2065, Horizon 2085 and beyond.

1.3 Definitions and Interpretation

This section helps you quickly find important words (key terms) used in the Constitution. It shows where each term is first explained or defined, and where the main rules about it are described. Use it to avoid confusion about what a term means or which parts of the Constitution apply to it.

Preamble: Establishes the mission, values, and intent of the Foundation.

-Article I: Definitions & Interpretation - Provides key terms, their meanings, and a cross-reference table for navigation.

-Article II: Legal Form, Registered Office & Objects - Defines the legal entity, registration, international expansion, and core purposes/objects of the Foundation.

Non-Profit Status and Asset Protection

-Article III: Non-Profit & No-Equity Policy - Enforces non-profit operations, prohibits equity distribution, and sets key prohibitions against private gain.

-Article XXV: Asset Lock & Perpetuity - Dedicates all assets irrevocably to the mission, defines assets, and ensures perpetual existence with enforcement mechanisms.

-Article XXVI: Severability, Saving Clause & Anti-Diversion of Foundation Assets - Includes severability for invalid provisions and prohibits diversion of assets to private ventures.

Membership and Participation

-Article IV: Members - Outlines membership types, rights, and termination.

-Article XXIII: Community & Beneficiary Participation - Mandates beneficiary advisory councils, community assemblies, digital feedback, and the Comrade Hub for co-creation and innovation.

Founder and Succession Mechanisms

-Article VI: Founder & Founder Privileges - Recognizes the founder's role, reserved powers, and explicit limitations.

-Article VII: Life Visionary Role & Limited Founder Powers (Founder Legacy Clause) - Defines the successor role, nomination via Family Protocol, powers, limitations, term, and removal.

Board and Executive Governance

-Article VIII: Board of Governors - Composition & Duties - Details board structure, appointment, duties, fiduciary responsibilities, and diversity requirements.

-Article IX: Board Committees - Establishes and mandates committees for audit, ethics, nominations, and remuneration.

-Article X: Executive Management - Covers CEO appointment, executive team, delegation, HR policy, remuneration, volunteers, and termination.

-Article XL: Meetings and Procedures - Regulates annual general meetings, notices, quorums, voting, extraordinary meetings, and board meetings

Staffing and Human Resources

-Article XI: Responsible Staffing & Anti-Ghost Worker Safeguards - Enforces lean staffing, headcount caps, payroll integrity, and anti-ghost worker measures.

-Article XI.10 - Volunteer Leadership & ADN Staffing Resolution - Clarifies PAN-Ambassador and Executive Directors as volunteer roles exempt from FTE caps with stipend-based compensation.

Funding and Financial Management

-Article V: Funding, Financial Controls & Revenue Flow - Manages primary funding from dividends, donations, mission fund allocation, controls, and reserves.

-Article V.A: Financial Disbursement Integrity & Anti-Leakage Mechanisms - Mandates direct digital transfers, beneficiary verification, staged disbursements, and zero-tolerance enforcement.

-Article XXXIII: Mission Investment & Non-Voting Share Protections - Regulates issuance of non-voting shares for mission-aligned capital with strict protections.

-Article XXXIV: Donations, Grants, Endowments & Contributions - Handles acceptance, restrictions, transparency, and anti-misuse of contributions.

-Article XLI: Investment and Endowment Management - Guides ethical investments, endowment protection, oversight, and policy review.

-Article LI: Funding Integrity and Non-Dilution - Prohibits diluting investments, defines permissible sources, requires contributor agreements, and sets reserve policies.

-Article LIII - Phased Funding & Direct Disbursement - Mandates centralized disbursement bypassing country-level intermediaries to prevent financial leakage.

Ownership, Control, and Operating Entities

-Article XXVIII: Ownership & Control Structure (Mission Protection Framework) - Outlines integrated model with trusts and companies, allocation principles, and safeguards.

-Article XXIX: Jowih Purpose Trust (Control Layer) - Establishes the irrevocable trust for voting control, trustees, powers, and limitations.

-Article XXX: Founder Family Trust (Legacy & Security Layer) - Sets up the trust for family economic shares, trustees, distributions, limitations and hold Golden Veto Power in operating companies to protect them from mission drift.

-Article XXXI: Operating Companies and Dividend Flow - Recognizes companies, enforces dividend splits, oversight, independence, and golden vote protections.

Procurement, Contracts, and Operations

-Article XII: Indemnification and Limitation of Liability - Provides protections for governors/officers, insurance, and related clauses.

-Article XIII: Procurement, Contracts & Partnerships - Sets principles for transparency, approvals, and conflict management in contracts.

-Article XVI: Intellectual Property & Branding - Governs IP ownership and licensing to protect mission assets.

-Article XVII: Risk, Compliance & Insurance - Requires risk registers, management policies, and insurance coverage.

Safeguarding and Beneficiary Focus

-Article XIV: Safeguarding, Child Protection & Beneficiary Selection - Commits to policies, selection principles, and complaint mechanisms.

-Article XXXVIII: Cultural Competence & Local Adaptation - Mandates training, FPIC consultations, localization, indigenous knowledge integration, and evaluations.

Technology, Data, and Infrastructure

-Article XV: Data Protection & Privacy - Establishes data governance and user transparency policies.

-Article XXXIX: Technology & Data Integrity (Modern Compliance) - Enforces responsible tech, compliance with laws, ethical AI, data sovereignty, and innovation.

-Article XLIX: Automation and Decentralized Governance - Prioritizes automation standards, governance, small-team models, and sovereignty.

-Article L: Distributed Infrastructure and Data Sovereignty - Sets standards for servers, redundancy, protection, and reporting.

Ethics, Compliance, and Anti-Corruption

-Article XXII: Ethics, Anti-Corruption & Sanctions - Defines prohibited conduct, reporting, investigations, sanctions, and reviews.

-Article XXVII: Conflict of Interest - Details duties, definitions, disclosure, recusal, prohibitions, and sanctions.

-Article XXXII: Legal Compliance & International Instruments - Ensures supremacy of law, adherence to human rights treaties, and harmonization.

-Article XXXVI: Whistleblower & Ethics Protection - Protects disclosures, prevents retaliation, and sets investigation timelines.

-Article XXXVII: Anti-Money Laundering & Counter-Terrorism Financing - Implements due diligence, sanctions compliance, reporting, and training.

-Article XLVII: Political Neutrality & Advocacy Limits - Enforces non-partisanship, permissible advocacy, expenditure limits, and enforcement.

Transparency, Audit, and Accountability

-Article XXXV: Transparency, Audit & Public Accountability - Principles for radical transparency, integrated audits, public dashboard, and reporting.

-Article XLII: Monitoring, Evaluation, and Impact Assessment - Establishes M&E frameworks, responsibilities, reporting, and improvement.

-Article XLIII: Audit Supremacy & The Independent Auditor General - Defines dual audits, IAG role, independence, powers, and cycles.

Amendments, Emergencies, and Dissolution

-Article XIX: Amendment Procedure  - Sets initiation, thresholds, tiered core articles, and safeguards.

-Article XX: Emergency Powers & Safeguards - Allows measures in existential threats with reporting requirements.

-Article XXI: Dissolution & Asset Distribution - Strict conditions for dissolution and asset transfer to similar entities.

-Article XLIV: The Public Referendum Process - Details triggers, eligibility, conduct, validity, and security for referendums.

-Article XLVI: Emergency Response & Continuity of Operations - Covers resilience, categories, safeguards, governance, and planning.

-Article XLVIII: Mission-Lock and Perpetual Alignment - Enforces unchanging mission adherence with mechanisms and integration.

Expansion, Acquisitions, and Jurisdiction

-Article XXIV: International Expansion & Jurisdiction - Manages branch registrations and conflict of laws.

-Article XLV: Strategic Acquisitions & Commercial Enterprise Alignment - Authorizes acquisitions with approvals, restructuring, prohibitions, and oversight.

-Article LII: Jurisdictional Flexibility - Strategies for multi-jurisdiction operations, structuring, transparency, and contingencies.

Dispute Resolution

-Article XVIII: Dispute Resolution - Requires internal resolution followed by external arbitration.

1.4 Core Article Status

This Article is designated as a Tier 1 Core Article 

Article II - Legal Form, Registered Office & Objects

2.1 Legal Form The Foundation is a Company Limited by Guarantee duly incorporated under the Companies Act 2001 (Mauritius) (or other chosen jurisdiction), has no share capital, and shall operate on a not-for-profit basis for the purposes set out in this Constitution. The Foundation shall register and maintain status as a Foundation under the Foundations Act 2012. As the mission-funding layer, it receives 90% of dividends from Jowih Holding Company for continental development use as in the mission and foundations subsidiaries budget.  

2.2 Registered Office There is no permanent principle office until Jowih Citadel is built. The Steward President  may from time to time decide on location of principle office in areas of its jurisdiction that align with protecting the foundation mission until such a time Jowih Citadel is fully built and the headquarter transferred. Until such a time, the principal office of the Foundation is in Port Louis, Mauritius. The Board may establish regional offices in other jurisdictions as stated in Article XXXVIII.  

2.3 International Expansion Authority  The Foundation may establish, affiliate, or partner with other foundations, trusts, or not-for-profit entities in any country for the purpose of expanding and coordinating mission-driven activities under the “Jowih” identity while maintaining independence and no merging.  

2.4 Global Governance Structure  

a) The Steward President, the two trusts may, by resolution, constitute a Global Jowih Foundation or similar umbrella body to coordinate international operations, governance, and reporting .  

b) Such global entity shall maintain the same mission, core principles, and ownership protections as set out in this Constitution.  

c) The Jowih Foundation (Mauritius) and any in other jurisdictions shall operate as a regional or national chapter under such global framework if established.

d) The Jowih Holding Company under the Jowih Foundation shall own all the foundation for profit companies and subsidiaries . It shall be registered in Mauritius.

e) The Jowih IP Company shall own all IPs of the foundation assets and operating companies and any subsidiary to ensure maximum protection and it shall be registered in Singapore for its great laws governing IP.  

2.5 Permanent Headquarters

The permanent headquarter shall be Jowih Citadel in tier zone 1 with the agreed country and shall server all official functions of the foundation locally and internationally.

2.6 Regional Offices & Compliance  

a) All regional and affiliated entities shall adopt and adhere to this Constitution, or to local equivalents approved by the Foundation that preserve its spirit and principles.  

b) Each regional office shall be subject to annual review by the IAG and the Oversight Panel for compliance with mission, ethics, and asset protection.  

2.7 International Cooperation  The Foundation may enter into cooperative agreements with trusted reputable  international organizations from Africa, African governments, or NGOs provided such agreements uphold the Foundation’s mission first, neutrality, non-political stance, and Core Articles.  

2.8 - OBJECTS AND PURPOSE - The Horizons 

2.8.1 The principal objects of the Foundation throughout the African continent and within the African diaspora are grouped into four thematic Pillars to ensure structured, strategic, and sustainable impact. All Objectives shall be executed within the GAG’s framework, supporting the continent's transition from foundational upliftment to technological leadership. Each Pillar advances the Mission through targeted, professional, and visionary initiatives, executed in a spirit of partnership with national governments where invited.

 PILLAR I - EMPOWER COMMUNITIES & BASIC NEEDSFocus: Elevating vulnerable populations through essential services and resilient infrastructure.

The Foundation shall:

(a)

Build a Continental Mega Orphanage , Mega STEM based University & Three strategic World Class state-of-art Hospitals across three regions, 10+Jowih international schools across marginalized areas primary and high schools across Africa in each country .(ii) Integrate comprehensive education from early childhood through high school with vocational training in partnered schools.(iii) Develop specialized curricula focusing on STEM standardazied across all Junior Secondary schools/high schools.(iv) Create transition programs for graduates into higher education or skilled employment

(v) Launch Mobile Hospital Infra program across all countries to cover areas limited with healthcare mostly.

(b) Community Infrastructure Development.

The foundation through its Africa Development Network (ADN) in partnership with local governments shall prioritize a community driven development strategy by involving community workforce and experts to :(i) Deploy modular affordable housing and build public storey buildings units using locally sourced, sustainable materials.                    This is aiming at renovating slums and eradicate slums across Africa hence better living conditions.(ii) Install Mega renewable energy micro-grids, including solar, wind, and hydro to boost energy in areas affected.(iii) Establish comprehensive healthcare clinics with telemedicine capabilities and smart health technologies.(iv) Implement climate-resilient agriculture systems with IoT monitoring and automated irrigation.(v) Develop clean water purification and distribution systems using solar-powered pumps.(vi) Create emergency relief protocols and stockpiles for natural disasters and crises.

(c) Strategic Partnerships & Infrastructure Expansion.(i) Collaborate with national governments as an execution and innovation partner on large-scale infrastructure projects.(ii) Partner with verified.reputable non-governmental organizations for specialized service delivery and capacity building.(iii) Work with institutional partners to scale proven interventions across multiple countries.(iv) Develop public-private partnerships for sustainable infrastructure maintenance.(v) Create  enterprises to ensure long-term economic sustainability.

 PILLAR II - DIGITAL OPPORTUNITY & YOUTH ADVANCEMENTFocus: Empowering African youth as innovators and competitive economic contributors.

The Foundation in collaborations with local governments shall:

(a) Education & Entrepreneurship Ecosystem.(i) Institute merit-based scholarships covering full tuition, accommodation, and living expenses.(ii) Develop specialized training programs in high-demand fields, including artificial intelligence, blockchain, robotics, and biotech.(iii) Create internship pathways .(iv) Establish startup seed funding with structured mentorship and incubation support under Jowih Venture Capital.(v) Build a state of art Mega university focused on STEM to boost Africa Innovation and talent pool and franchise across partnered universities across Africa.

(b) Mission Fund Administration & Revenue Streams.(i) Administer and oversee the Mission Fund with transparent allocation mechanisms.(ii) Manage revenue from digital platforms, philanthropic contributions, etc.(iii) Receive and allocate funds from the Phased funding program in the foundation from the Holding Company(v) Implement rigorous financial controls and public reporting on fund utilization.

(c) Ancillary Support Activities.(i) Undertake research and development to enhance program effectiveness.(ii) Establish monitoring and evaluation frameworks for impact assessment.(iii) Create knowledge management systems for best practice sharing.(iv) Develop capacity building programs for local implementation partners.

(d) Digital Platform Development & Deployment.(i) Develop and deploy advanced digital platforms and Jowih COS for mass accessibility.(ii) Establish vocational digital facilities with state-of-the-art equipment.(iii) Create online academies with localized content in multiple African languages.(iv) Implement digital literacy programs reaching rural and marginalized communities.(v) Develop programming skills training from basic coding to advanced software engineering.

(e) Pan-African Animation & Gaming Hubs.(i) Establish a continental network of regional hubs specializing in digital creative industries.(ii) Develop talent pipelines through accredited animation and gaming academies.(iii) Create original African intellectual property rooted in continental heritage and future visions.(iv) Build technology infrastructure with state-of-the-art studios and rendering farms.(v) Foster economic ecosystems through startup incubation and global distribution.(vi) Ensure cultural preservation by documenting folklore through interactive media.(vii) Achieve technological sovereignty by developing African game engines and tools.

(f) Africa Internet Accessibility. 

(i)  Forge the "Africa Connect Alliance" through partnerships with telecommunications providers and relevant government agencies across the continent, enabling affordable, inclusive access to the Jowih COS's core features for all Africans.

(ii) For a modest fixed monthly fee as agreed (calibrated to local economic tiers), Telcos shall unlock agreed features not limited to; text-only communication via  chat and  wallet transactions, ensuring seamless participation in forums, Development Assemblies hubs,micro- trade, and development opportunities without data barriers.

 (iii) This initiative upholds the principle of leaving no one behind, democratizing digital connectivity for underserved rural and low-income users, fostering Ubuntu-inspired unity, and measuring success through metrics like user adoption rates and cross-border collaboration growth.

(g) Establish Vencture Capital Program to seed startups into the Jowih ecosystem and support both non-profit startups and for-profit startups across Africa.

PILLAR III - AFRICAN RESEARCH & INNOVATION LEADERSHIPFocus: Catalyzing Africa's transition into an innovation-driven economy.

The Foundation shall:

Enterprise Development & Supervision.(i) Establish social-impact enterprises focusing on scalable solutions for African challenges.(ii) Incorporate mission-aligned companies with clear social return metrics.(iii) Own and supervise enterprises ensuring adherence to Foundation values and ethics.(iv) Create spin-off companies from successful research and development projects.(v) Implement governance structures that balance innovation with mission alignment.

(B) Strategic Acquisitions & Restructuring.(i) Identify and acquire established commercial enterprises with transformation potential.(ii) Restructure acquired companies to adopt Foundation governance and ownership models.(iii) Implement a one-hundred-and-eighty (180) day Mission Alignment Period for all acquisitions, as stipulated in Article XLV.(iv) Ensure acquired companies contribute to the Pan-African innovation ecosystem.(v) Create vertical integration across technology value chains.

(C) Research Hubs & Open-Source Technologies.(i) AI and Technology R&D(ii) Health R&D(iii) Agritech R&D(iv)  Energy R&D(v) Mining R&D(vi) Water R&D(vii) Infrastructure R&D

(D) Diaspora Expertise Mobilization and Bond investment.(i) Create virtual collaboration platforms connecting global African experts with local projects.(ii) Establish structured mentorship programs with clear knowledge transfer protocols.(iii) Develop incentive systems for sustainable knowledge repatriation and investment through Bonds.(iv) Build digital workspaces enabling remote contribution to African innovation.(v) Create recognition and reward systems for diaspora contributions.

PILLAR IV - THE FUTURE CIVILIZATION (CYBERPUNK AFRICA VISION)

Focus: Positioning Africa to shape the future of global civilization through diversified, sovereign partnerships.

The Foundation shall:

A. Africa-Centric Advanced Technology Development

(i) Design and produce affordable smartphones with features tailored for African users .

(ii) Develop electric vehicles and supporting charging infrastructure suited for African conditions and powered by renewable energy.

(iii) Create augmented and virtual reality platforms for education, cultural preservation, and virtual tourism.

(iv) Deploy service, industrial, and companion robots across healthcare, manufacturing, and daily life.

(v) Develop sovereign AI infrastructure for healthcare, education, and economic planning.

(vi) Establish ethical AI governance frameworks ensuring alignment with African principles and community values.

(vii) Build New Dawn City as futuristic city and SEZ after the famous “Shenzhen city” which after can be replicated across selected Capital cities across Africa.

B. Strategic Future Partnerships & Investments

The Foundation shall execute a diversified, multi-polar strategy of strategic partnerships and minority stakes to rapidly acquire technological capability and market influence, leveraging its core strategic assets while avoiding dependency on any single foreign power.

(I) Space and Orbital Infrastructure

a) The Foundation shall secure board representation and strategic stakes in low-earth orbit satellite constellations from multiple global providers to ensure redundancy and competitive pricing.

b) Primary partners shall include:

Starlink or OneWeb (United States) - for primary bandwidth and proven deployment

China Satellite Communications (China Satcom) or CGWIC (China) - for alternative coverage and technology transfer

IRIS² (European Union) - for regulatory alignment and sovereign European access

c) The objective is to guarantee primary bandwidth for the Foundation's remote education, telemedicine, and IoT networks across the African continent while maintaining negotiating leverage through diversified contracts.

(II) Semiconductor Sovereignty

a) The Foundation's initial stake in NVIDIA Corporation shall be balanced with strategic stakes in:

HiSilicon or SMIC (China) - for access to alternative architectures

Graphcore (United Kingdom) - for European AI compute diversification

b) The Foundation shall partner with and take strategic stakes in specialized fabless design houses from multiple jurisdictions.

c) Furthermore, the Foundation shall lead a consortium to build an Assembly, Test, and Packaging (ATP) facility in Africa, partnering with industry leaders from both Western and Eastern sources (e.g., ASE Group (Taiwan) or JCET (China)).

(III) Hyperscale Data Centers

a) The Foundation shall partner with and acquire strategic stakes in African data center leaders, 

b) The Foundation  shall develop new build-to-suit hyperscale campuses powered by dedicated renewable micro-grids from  knowledge-transfer programs across diversified partners globally :

c) The foundation shall invest and scale existing data centers within Africa to meet global standards as part of it’s partnership.

(IV) Climate and Water Resilience Technologies

a) The Foundation shall partner with and acquire stakes in leading climate analytics firms from multiple regions, including:

ClimaCell Inc. (United States)

China Meteorological Administration commercial partners (China)

European Centre for Medium-Range Weather Forecasts (ECMWF) (Europe)

(V) Metaverse and Digital Twins

a) The Foundation shall leverage its strategic partnership with NVIDIA Corporation (United States) for early access to the NVIDIA Omniverse platform, while also exploring:

Baidu's XiRang (China) - for Asian metaverse interoperability

Open-source alternatives (Europe/Global) - for vendor independence

b) The Foundation shall partner with and invest in African gaming and virtual reality studios to build continent-specific digital twins and virtual experiences.

c) The objective is to create the foundational digital-twin infrastructure for African cities and heritage sites, to be owned and operated by the Foundation.

(VI) Quantum Technologies

a) The Foundation shall establish research partnerships with leading quantum entities from multiple jurisdictions:

IBM Quantum, D-Wave Systems Inc. (United States)

University of Science and Technology of China (USTC), QuantumCTek (China)

IQM Quantum Computers (Finland/Europe)

b) The Foundation shall take initial stakes in quantum computing software startups from Africa, China, Europe, and the United States.

c) The objective is to build a dedicated quantum research group within the Foundation's artificial intelligence research hubs, focusing on optimization problems in logistics and drug discovery, while maintaining access to multiple quantum ecosystems.

(VII) Biocomputing and Neurotechnology

a) The Foundation shall partner with and acquire minor stakes in frontier research companies from multiple regions:

Final (US) and Altos Labs (US/UK) - for longevity

Synchron (US/Australia) and Neuralink (US) - for brain-computer interface technology

NeuroXess (China) - for Asian neurotech diversification

NeuroElectrics (Spain/Europe) - for European regulatory alignment

b) The objective is to establish a dedicated bio-digital research division to explore applications in personalized medicine and advanced human-computer interfaces for Africa, with ethical oversight and diversified supply chains.

(VIII) Artificial Intelligence & Sovereign Compute

a) The Foundation shall establish AI research partnerships across multiple jurisdictions:

OpenAI, Anthropic, Google DeepMind (United States) - for frontier models

Baidu, Alibaba DAMO Academy, SenseTime (China),DeepSeek - for Asian AI applications

Aleph Alpha (Germany/Europe) - for European sovereign AI

b) The Foundation shall build Africa's first sovereign AI supercomputing center, using hardware from multiple sources (NVIDIA + Huawei Ascend + European) to ensure supply chain resilience.

(IX) Global Innovation Leadership

(i) Establish Africa as a net exporter of technology and intellectual property.

(ii) Create African technology standards for global adoption.

(iii) Develop thought leadership in ethical technology governance.

(iv) Build Africa's capacity to shape global technological evolution.

(v) Ensure African perspectives are central to twenty-second century civilization development.

(vi) Maintain strategic non-alignment - partner with all.

2.8.4: THE PARALLEL SOVEREIGNTY STRATEGY

2.8.4.1 The Foundation shall pursue three concurrent and mutually reinforcing strategies to achieve technological sovereignty:

Strategy

Description

Purpose

Acquire

Procure best-in-class technology from global partners for immediate deployment

Accelerate development without delay

Adapt

Establish co-development, technology transfer, and local customization agreements

Build indigenous capacity

Advance

Invest in African-led R&D, sovereign infrastructure, and homegrown innovation

Create long-term independence

2.8.4.2 These strategies shall operate in parallel, not in sequence. The Foundation shall not delay deployment while waiting for local capacity, nor shall it become perpetually dependent on foreign sources.

2.8.4.3 Strategic Non-Alignment

The Foundation shall maintain partnerships across multiple global jurisdictions - including but not limited to the United States, China, the European Union, India, Brazil, and others.

2.8.4.4 The Exchange Principle

All technology partnerships shall be structured as mutually beneficial exchanges. The Foundation contributes market access, African talent development, collaborative research opportunities, and long-term operational partnerships. In return, it secures technology transfer, joint intellectual property, training, and equitable equity arrangements.

2.8.4.5 Redundancy 

For each strategic technology category (semiconductors, space, artificial intelligence, cloud infrastructure, quantum computing, neurotechnology), the Foundation shall:

(a) Maintain at least two independent foreign sources; and

(b) Maintain a parallel African development track.

When African alternatives achieve functional parity, foreign procurement shall be reduced accordingly - but never eliminated entirely, to preserve competitive pressure and supply chain resilience.

2.8.4.6 Continental Sovereignty as a Spectrum

The Foundation recognizes that technological sovereignty is not a binary state but a spectrum. The goal is continuous progress toward greater African ownership, control, and capacity - not absolute independence overnight.

ASSET LOCK 

The assets of the Foundation are irrevocably dedicated to its Mission under the principles and mechanisms detailed in Article XXV (Asset Lock & Perpetuity).

2.8.2 Cross-Pillar Integration & ImplementationAll Pillars shall operate in coordinated synergy, with shared infrastructure, talent mobility, and integrated planning. The Foundation shall ensure that basic needs fulfillment under Pillar I creates stable foundations for digital advancement under Pillar II, which fuels research innovation under Pillar III, ultimately positioning Africa for civilizational leadership under Pillar IV.

2.8.3: Strategic Non-Alignment & Diversification Principle

The Foundation acknowledges that early strategic partnerships shall rapidly boost Pillar IV objectives before achieving strategic independence  and therefore: 

2.8.3.1 The Foundation shall pursue a multi-polar, diversified partnership strategy across all Pillar IV initiatives.

2.8.3.2 No single local or  foreign country, bloc, or corporation shall hold a dominant or exclusive position in any critical technology partnership.

2.8.3.3 The Foundation shall maintain, where feasible, at least two independent sources for each strategic technology category (e.g., space, semiconductors, cloud, AI, quantum).

2.8.3.4 The Foundation shall prioritize technology transfer, joint venture, and local manufacturing agreements over pure import arrangements.

2.8.3.5 The Foundation shall reserve the right to terminate any partnership that imposes restrictions on African technological sovereignty.

2.8.3.6 The Foundation's partnerships shall be guided by the principle: "Africa First, Global Best."

2.9 Asset Lock The assets of the Foundation are irrevocably dedicated to its Mission under the principles and mechanisms detailed in Article XXV (Asset Lock & Perpetuity).  

2.10 Core Article Status

This Article is designated as a Tier 1 Core Article 

ARTICLE III - NON-PROFIT & NO-EQUITY POLICY

3.1 Non-Profit Status

The Foundation shall not distribute profits, dividends, or capital to any individual, Trustee, officer, employee, or Member.

All surpluses, including operating surpluses, investment gains, and proceeds from any disposition of assets, must be reinvested to further the Foundation's objects under Article II.

No part of the Foundation's income or assets shall inure to the benefit of any private person or entity.

The Foundation entity owns 90% economic interest of the Holding company with zero percent voting power or board seats and this absolute is absolute. Any violation is a Mission-Threatening Action under this constitution.

3.2 No Equity Rights

No officer, Trustee, Member, employee, contractor, or any external entity shall possess any equity, ownership, or shareholding rights in the Foundation.

The Foundation shall not:

(a) List or issue shares of any kind;

(b) Create or recognize any instrument that confers ownership rights;

(c) Sell, transfer, or assign any ownership interest in the Foundation;

(d) Merge or consolidate with any entity in a manner that would confer ownership rights on any person.

3.3 Restriction on Purpose Trust Equity

The Jowih Purpose Trust holds a sixty percent (60%) voting interest in Jowih Holdings Limited and serves as the perpetual mission guardian (Article XXIX). While Founder Trust holds 10% economic rights and 40% voting rights for mission continuity. They ensure the Foundation receives it’s 90% economic rights;

The Jowih Purpose Trust is strictly and irrevocably prohibited from selling, transferring, assigning, pledging, or encumbering its voting interest or any portion thereof to any person or entity for any reason whatsoever.

Any purported sale, transfer, assignment, pledge, or encumbrance in violation of this prohibition is void ab initio and constitutes a Mission-Threatening under this constitution.

This prohibition exists because:

(a) The Purpose Trust's 60% voting interest is the foundation of mission control;

(b) Any third-party ownership of the Purpose Trust's voting interest would enable mission capture, dilution, or destruction;

(c) The Purpose Trust must remain perpetually under mission-aligned trustees who owe fiduciary duties exclusively to the Mission.

No amendment to this Constitution may ever authorize the sale or transfer of the Purpose Trust's voting interest. This provision is permanently unamendable.

3.4 Restriction on Family Trust Equity

The Founder Family Trust holds a ten percent (10%) economic interest & 40% voting rights in Jowih Holdings Limited and the Golden Vote (Article XXX.4) as the ultimate safeguard against mission-threatening actions.

The Founder Family Trust is strictly and irrevocably prohibited from selling, transferring, assigning, pledging, or encumbering its equity interest,voting rights or the Golden Vote to any person or entity, including family members, for any reason whatsoever.Any purported sale, transfer, assignment, pledge, or encumbrance in violation of this prohibition is void ab initio and constitutes a Mission-Threatening Action under this constitution.

This prohibition exists because:

(a) The Golden Vote is critical to maintaining mission integrity and preventing mission-threatening actions;

(b) Any third-party ownership of the Family Trust's equity would introduce an unacceptable risk of mission compromise, capture, or dilution;

(c) The separation of mission control (Purpose Trust) and economic benefit (Family Trust) must remain inviolate.

No amendment to this Constitution may ever authorize the sale or transfer of the Family Trust's equity or the Golden Vote. This provision is permanently unamendable.

3.5 Asset Lock Integration

All Foundation assets are subject to the Asset Lock under Article XXV. No asset may be sold, transferred, or encumbered for private enrichment.

Any purported sale, transfer, or encumbrance in violation of this Article is void ab initio.

3.6 Prohibitions

The Foundation shall not:

(a) Permit any private person or entity to acquire ownership rights in the Foundation or any of its material assets;

(b) Pledge, mortgage, or otherwise encumber the Mission Fund or any material Foundation asset for the purpose of generating private equity, securing loans for private benefit, or any purpose not directly serving the Mission;

(c) Engage in activities contrary to the Mission as set forth in Article II;

(d) Significantly divert funds away from Mission-related projects except as expressly permitted by Article V (Funding, Financial Controls & Revenue Flow);

(e) Provide loans, guarantees, or credit support to any private person or entity, except as expressly permitted for mission-aligned purposes under Article XXXIII (Mission Investment & Non-Voting Share Protections);

(f) Use Foundation assets to support or oppose political candidates or parties, as provided in Article XLVII.

3.7 No Private Inurement

No part of the Foundation's net earnings shall inure to the benefit of any private person, including the Founder, the Founder's family, Trustees, officers, or Members.

Compensation for services actually rendered shall be reasonable and shall not constitute inurement.

Reimbursement for legitimate expenses incurred on Foundation business shall not constitute inurement.

3.8 Core Article Status

This Article is designated as a Tier 1 Core Article under Article XIX.3 and may be amended only through the procedures therein.

ARTICLE IV - MEMBERS

4.1 Membership Categories

The Foundation shall have two categories of Members: Founding Member and Ordinary Members.

The Founder, Reuben Oduk, is the sole Founding Member. No other person, including any other signatory to this Constitution, shall be deemed a Founding Member. The title of Founding Member is personal to Reuben Oduk and expires upon his death or permanent incapacity.This is to do away with any hostile decisions or takeovers that can threaten the mission.

Ordinary Members may be admitted by Board resolution subject to rules established by the Board but do not have power to control and direct the foundation on what to do or amend this constitution.

4.2 Rights of Members

Members do not hold equity, shares, or ownership rights in the Foundation.

Members may:

(a) Attend and vote at general meetings in accordance with membership rules;

(b) Participate in referendums under Article XLIV where provided;

(c) Receive information published for Members.

4.3 Termination of Membership

Membership may be terminated by resignation, death, incapacity, or by unanimous Board determination for cause, following procedural fairness.

 4.4 Core Article Status

This Article is designated as a Tier 1 Core Article under Article XIX.3 and may be amended only through the procedures therein.

Article V - Funding, Financial Controls & Revenue Flow  

5.1 Primary Funding Source  The Foundation's primary revenue shall be the initial received Bond, the designated portion(s) of dividends from the Jowih Holding Company receiving not less than 90% of the profits,  allocated to the Foundation as agreed by the Operating Companies and the Foundation ("Jowih Co. - Foundation Remittance Agreement"). The remaining 10% of dividends from Jowih Holding Company shall flow to the Founder Family Trust for family security. 

5.2 Donation Handling  All Community Charity Donations from regular citizens or comrades (Paybill, QR, wire transfers) and Major Donations from philanthropists must be received into Foundation bank accounts under the Foundation's legal name and logged in the immutable public ledger within 48 hours.

.5.3 Mission Fund & Allocation  

a) The Mission Fund is a ring-fenced fund used only for mission activities under The GAG. 

b) The Foundation led by Stewardship Office to adopt an annual budget allocating precise percentages to core areas (e.g., operations, pilot projects, reserves) but shall prioritize seed and capital expenditures required to scale social programs.  

c) The Foundation shall maintain a separate Strategic Acquisition Reserve funded from specifically designated sources, not to exceed 20% of the Foundation's total assets, to be used exclusively for mission-aligned acquisitions under Article XLV.  

5.4 Financial Controls  

a) All expenditures above a defined threshold require two signatories: IAG and Steward President .  

b) Procurement above threshold requires open tender or competitive bidding; exceptions require an Audit Committee waiver with public rationale.  

c) Quarterly and annual audited financial statements are mandatory.  

5.5 Reserve The Foundation shall maintain a reserve (operational buffer) 

5.6 Core Article Status

This Article is designated as a Tier 1 Core Article under Article XIX.3 and may be amended only through the procedures therein.

Article V.A - Financial Disbursement Integrity & Anti-Leakage Mechanisms

V.A.1 - Principle of Direct Digital TransferAll disbursements of program funds, grants, stipends, community-project payments, and operational field expenditures shall be executed exclusively through Foundation-authorized digital payment infrastructure through the Governance OS, including but not limited to the Jowih COS Wallet System, approved Payment Service Providers (PSPs), and secure institutional transfer channels.

No cash-based disbursement, manual handling, off-system payment, or use of unverified third-party intermediaries is permitted. Any such attempt or occurrence constitutes a Mission-Threatening Action subject to Tier-1 enforcement and sanctions under Articles XXII (Anti-Corruption & Sanctions) and XXV (Asset Lock & Perpetuity).

A temporary cash exception may only be permitted where digital infrastructure is demonstrably absent, and solely under all of the following controls:

(a) A unanimous Combined Trust Board resolution authorizing the exception;(b) Activation of an Enhanced Oversight Protocol, including real-time two-party validation and field audit tracking;(c) A full forensic reconciliation logged and certified by the Independent Auditor General within thirty (30) days.

Failure to comply with this Article automatically triggers immediate disbursement suspension and investigative action as defined under Article XXII.

V.A.2 - Multi-Factor Beneficiary VerificationNo disbursement shall occur until beneficiaries are validated through at least two (2) independent verification factors chosen from:(a) Government-issued ID, mobile-KYC, or biometric data;(b) Geo-tagged site validation;(c) Digital activity logs demonstrating engagement or milestone progress;(d) Written validation by a Trusted Local Partner as defined in Article XXIII;(e) System-based duplicate beneficiary screening.The Foundation shall maintain a Unified Beneficiary Registry to prevent duplication and fraud.

V.A.3 - Performance-Based Tranche DisbursementsFor community-led initiatives and large-scale programs, funds shall be released in structured tranches, contingent upon:(a) Verified completion of agreed milestones;(b) Positive progress audits by the Independent Auditor General (IAG) or accredited third-party;(c) Real-time digital reporting of progress metrics;(d) Confirmation of proper utilization of prior tranches;(e) Local verification by Beneficiary Advisory Councils where established.Failure to meet conditions automatically suspends subsequent disbursements pending independent review.

V.A.4 - Immutable Transparency & Public Audit TrailsEach disbursement shall produce:(a) A cryptographically-secured or Hashed, time-stamped audit trail retrievable by the IAG;(b) Automated receipts delivered to recipients and archived in the Data Integrity Ledger;(c) Public transparency reporting through the Public Dashboard under Article XXXV;(d) Automated monitoring alerts for anomalies indicative of fraud, leakage, or corruption.All digital records shall be preserved for a minimum of fifteen (15) years.

V.A.5 - Zero-Tolerance EnforcementThe following constitute Gross Misconduct under Article XXII and shall trigger immediate sanctions:(a) Creation or maintenance of ghost beneficiaries or fictitious claims;(b) Unauthorized diversion, skimming, or leakage of funds;(c) Falsification of verification data, milestones, or reporting;(d) Manual manipulation or bypassing of digital controls;(e) Repeated failure to comply with documentation requirements.Upon detection, the CEO and IAG must immediately:(i) freeze all related disbursements;(ii) launch a forensic audit;(iii) seek full recovery of funds; and(iv) pursue disciplinary, civil, and criminal proceedings.

V.A.6 - Integrated Audit MandateThe Independent Auditor General shall conduct periodic compliance testing of these mechanisms under Article XLIII, including surprise audits.The External Audit Firm shall issue a specific annual opinion on:(a) effectiveness of anti-leakage controls; and(b) any material weaknesses requiring remediation.All findings shall be summarized in the Annual Impact Report.

V.A.7 - Technological Safeguards & Continuous ImprovementThe Foundation shall deploy and maintain secure financial infrastructure that includes:(a) multi-factor authenticated payment gateways;(b) machine-learning-based anomaly and pattern detection;(c) lawful integration with national identity or verification systems;(d) encryption standards meeting global best practice;(e) regular cyber-security penetration testing by certified external specialists;(f) contingency systems to ensure continuity during outages.The CTO, in collaboration with the IAG, shall annually certify that technological safeguards remain fit-for-mission.

 Core Article Status

This Article is designated as a Tier 2 Core Article under Article XIX.3 and may be amended only through the procedures therein.

ARTICLE VI - THE STEWARD PRESIDENT

6.1 Founder Recognition & Nature of Role

The Founder, Reuben Oduk, is hereby recognized as the Founder & Steward President (Permanent Guardian) of the Jowih Foundation. This is a permanent, lifetime, and non-transferable role designed to be the supreme guardian of the Foundation's mission, vision, and constitutional integrity. The role symbolizes the moral and inspirational foundation of the Jowih Foundation. The Founder cannot be removed from office by any body, board, Trust, or referendum. The Founder may only voluntarily step down to allow the Second Steward President (Life Visionary) to take over. The title "Founder & Steward President" is unique to the Founder and does not transfer. Upon the Founder's voluntary step down, death, or certified permanent incapacity, the guardian powers of this office transfer to and are exercised by the Life Visionary as stipulated in Article VII.

6.2 Supreme Guardian Powers (Reserved & Substantive)

Subject to applicable law, these Articles, and the overriding fiduciary duties outlined in Article VIII.4, the Steward President (whether the First Steward President Founder or subsequent Steward Presidents) shall have the following reserved powers during their tenure. The Steward President does not engage in day-to-day operations of the Foundation or any Zone. His/Her role is strategic oversight, mission guardianship, and final arbitration. He acts only when the Mission is threatened or when subordinate officers cannot resolve disputes. When he acts, his decision is final and binding.These powers are designed to preserve mission integrity through substantive oversight without day-to-day operational control:

(a) Right to Strategic Oversight & Reporting: The right to receive comprehensive high-level reports from the Foundation CEO, the Chairs of the Oversight Committee and IAG office, and the Holding Company Board at least quarterly.

(b) Right to Consultation on Strategic Direction: The Steward President shall be consulted in advance on major strategic decisions, including but not limited to the adoption of new mission areas, significant partnerships exceeding defined thresholds, or changes to the annual budget that materially affect core programs. The Board shall provide written documentation and consider the input in good faith.

(c)Executive Appointment & Removal: Sole power to appoint and, for cause, remove the Foundation's Chief Executive Officer (CEO) and subsidiary company CEOs.Removal of CEOs by subsequent Steward President must be ratified by the Holding Company Board.

(d) First Generation Oversight Committee Appointment: The First Steward President (Founder) has the sole power to appoint the first generation members of the independent Oversight Committee as provided in Article IX.1. Subsequent appointments follow Article IX.1.

(e) Limited Veto Right on Core Matters: A limited veto right on proposed amendments to Core Articles as defined in Article XIX.3 and on any Board resolution deemed to constitute material mission drift. The First Steward President's (Founder) veto is absolute and cannot be overridden by any body, board, or Trust. For subsequent Steward Presidents (Second, Third, etc.), the veto may be overridden only by a three quarters vote of the Combined Trusts Board (Purpose Trust and Family Trust acting jointly) after a mandatory reconsideration period.

(f) Right to Chair the Steward's Council: Shall serve as the non-executive Chair of the Steward's Council. For the First Steward President (Founder), this Council is designated the "Founder's Council." For subsequent Steward Presidents, it is designated the "Foundation’s Advisory Council."

(g) Right to Nominate Board Members: May nominate or recommend candidates for Board positions, subject to standard confirmation processes.

(h) High-Level Representation & Engagement: Represents the Foundation at the continental and global level in all matters of strategic,diplomatic, and institutional significance. The Steward President leads and presides over such engagements, including major announcements, partnerships, and defining milestones of the Foundation.The Foundation CEO and other officers may accompany and support such engagements as appropriate. In the absence of the Steward President,representation may be delegated, but such delegation does not transfer the Steward President’s authority. 

(i) First Generation Appointments: The First Steward President (Founder) has the additional power to appoint the initial Trustees of the Jowih Purpose Trust as provided in Article XXIX.2, the initial Trustees of the Founder Family Trust as provided in Article XXX.2, the first generation IAG and any other first generation appointments . These first-generation appointment powers are personal to the Founder and do not transfer to subsequent Steward Presidents.

(I) Holding Company Governance Rights: The Founder shall retain the following exclusive rights regarding Jowih Holdings Limited and its subsidiaries:

(II) First CEO Appointments: Exclusive right to appoint the inaugural CEO of each new Operating Subsidiary. This appointment requires Holding Company Board ratification but cannot be unreasonably withheld.

(III) Cultural Principles: Authority to define up to five (5) "Legacy Cultural Principles" that must be incorporated into all subsidiary governance documents.

(IV) Strategic Veto: Right to veto any Holding Company strategic plan that fundamentally contradicts the Founder's original vision as documented in the Vision Charter.

(V) Board Attendance: Right to attend any Holding Company or subsidiary board meeting as a non-voting observer.

(j) Power to Appoint Special Advisors: The Steward President may appoint Special Advisors to provide expert counsel on specific strategic areas. Special Advisors serve at the Steward President's pleasure,have no independent authority, and are not constitutional officers.Their roles, duties, and salary shall be defined in operational documents or Board resolutions, not in this Constitution.

(k) Appointment of New Dawn City Administrator: Sole power to appoint and,for cause, remove the New Dawn City Administrator as provided in Article LVII.2(c)(x) and (xi).

6.3 Founder-Specific Privileges (Non-Transferable)

The following privileges are personal to the First Steward President (Founder) and do not transfer to any successor:

(a) Direct Control of Family Trust: The Founder maintains direct control over the Founder Family Trust as provided in Article XXX. Successors have no authority over the Family Trust.

(b) No Salary: The Founder receives no salary for holding the office of Steward President. Subsequent Steward Presidents shall  receive salary as determined by the Remuneration Committee not less than $1M as the Steward is not allowed to external office or job.This is to also provide stable financial support to the family.

(c) Unique Title: The title "Founder & Steward President" is personal to the Founder and is never used by any successor.

(d) Critical First Generation Appointments: The power to make first generation appointments across Foundation and other organs as provided in this Constitution, including the first generation Oversight Committee and initial Trustees of both Trusts.

(e) Cannot Be Removed: The Founder cannot be removed from office by any body, board, Trust, or referendum. The Founder may only voluntarily step down.

(f) Chair Founder council which siezes to exist after the founder no longer exist.

(g) Absolute veto: Founder veto is absolute.Incoming subsequent Steward president veto can be vetoed as in article 6.2 of this constitution.

(h) Appointment of Independent Auditor General & Trustees Oversight Panel:The first appointments shall be made by the Founder and subsequents appointed as required in this constitution.No subsequent Steward President has power to do these specific appointments.

6.4 Explicit Limitations

The Steward President's powers are strictly limited:

(a) No Override of Legal or Fiduciary Duties.

(b) Limited Operational Mandate: Shall not interfere in day-to-day operations unless necessary to prevent mission drift.

(c) Transparency Requirement: All exercises of power shall be documented and published.

(d) The Life Visionary does not appoint the Independent Auditor General. The IAG is appointed by unanimous vote of both Trusts as provided in Article XLIII.2 when the founder is not around.

6.5 No Deputy Steward President

There is no office of "Deputy Steward President," "Vice Steward President," or any similar title. No person shall act in the place of the Steward President except the Steward President personally. In the event of the Steward President's temporary incapacity, the Foundation CEO may exercise emergency administrative functions but shall not exercise the Steward President's reserved powers under Section 6.2. This prohibition is permanent and any attempt to breach such power shall be treated as Mission Threatening Action immediately.

6.6 Integration with Succession

Upon the Founder's voluntary step down, death, or permanent incapacity, all powers and authorities stipulated in Section 6.2 of this Article (excluding Founder-specific privileges under Section 6.3) shall be inherited by the subsequent Steward President honored as “Life Visionary”, who shall exercise them in full while remaining bound by the limitations in Section 6.4.

6.7 Diplomatic Standards and Protocol Integrity

(a) All engagements involving the Steward President or the Foundation CEO shall adhere to standards of parity, efficiency, and institutional dignity.

(b) The Foundation shall not participate in any engagement where:

    (i) Access is delayed without operational justification;

    (ii) Representation is segregated or deprioritized in a manner inconsistent with peer participants;

    (iii) Logistical arrangements diminish the independence, security, or status of the office.

(c) Movement and access for the Steward President and the Foundation CEO shall be direct, secure, and commensurate with the level of engagement.

(d) The Foundation reserves the right to withdraw from or decline any engagement where protocol standards are not met.

(e) These standards apply equally across all jurisdictions, institutions, and host entities without exception.

6.8 Review and Amendment

This Article is a Tier 1 Core Article under Article XIX.3 and may only be amended as prescribed therein, with the Founder & Steward President's consent required during their lifetime.

ARTICLE VII - THE LIFE VISIONARY (SUCCESSOR STEWARD PRESIDENT)

7.1 Purpose & Role

The Life Visionary is the custodian of the Founder's legacy and the designated successor to exercise the stewardship guardianship powers. The Life Visionary is not a deputy, vice, or subordinate. The Life Visionary is the successor who, upon the Founder's voluntary step down, death, or permanent incapacity, becomes the Steward President. For purposes of numbering, the Founder is the First Steward President. The Life Visionary who succeeds the Founder shall be the Second Steward President. Each subsequent successor shall be numbered consecutively (Third, Fourth, Fifth, etc.). The honorific "Life Visionary" may be used alongside the numbered title.

7.2 Designation

Upon the Founder's voluntary step down, death, or permanent incapacity, or upon the expiry of a successor's term, the Founder's family, as defined in the Family Protocol and confidential ANNEX A, shall follow the nomination procedure outlined therein. The detailed procedures for succession (upon Founder's step down, death, or incapacity) and planned succession (after a Life Visionary's term ends) are distinguished and fully set forth in ANNEX A.

7.3 Assumption of Stewardship Powers

Upon the Founder's voluntary step down, death, or permanent incapacity, the Life Visionary shall immediately inherit and may exercise all the powers and responsibilities of the Steward President as defined in Article VI.2 (Supreme Guardian Powers), excluding the Founder-specific privileges under Article VI.3, and is bound by the limitations in Article VI.4. The successor exercises these powers under the title of Steward President (numbered Second, Third, etc.), with the honorific "Life Visionary."
