(i) Presentation of prioritized CDE projects(ii) Presentation of County Government development plans(iii) Identification of overlaps and inefficiencies(iv) Alignment of execution efforts(v) Workforce coordination

(e) Government–CDE Collaboration

Government and CDE shall jointly:

Align development priorities

Merge overlapping projects

Deploy CDE workforce to accelerate execution

Improve efficiency and reduce delays

(f) Execution Principle

All projects must have defined timelines.Long-duration projects shall be restructured to significantly reduce delivery time, with a strong preference for completion within three (3) years or less.

(g) Binding Output

Each CDA shall produce a County Development Concord, including:

Approved CDE projects (ranked by citizen priority)

Government project alignment

Budget allocations

Execution responsibility (CDE, Government, or Joint)

Workforce deployment

Fixed timelines

54.8.5 National Development Assembly 

(a) VenueThe  meeting shall be held in a large public arena.

(b) Attendance

(i) PAN-Ambassador (Chair)(ii) Invited President or Prime Minister(iii) National Ministers(iv) CDE Delegates

(c) Core Functions

(i) Presentation of national government development agenda(ii) Presentation of aggregated CDE priorities(iii) Allocation and coordination of the Country Budget Pot(iv) Alignment of nationwide and cross-county projects

(d) Binding Output

The NDA shall produce a National Development Concord, defining national priorities, funding structures, and execution timelines.

54.8.6 Disbursement and Execution

(a) Funds shall be disbursed based on approved Concords.

(b) Joint execution shall enable governments to utilize CDE workforce capacity to:

Accelerate project delivery

Reduce timelines

Improve efficiency

54.8.7 Accountability and Transparency

(a) All projects shall have fixed timelines.

(b) Failure to meet timelines without valid justification shall result in:

(i) Withdrawal of remaining funds(ii) Reallocation to higher-priority projects

(c) All project data, progress, and timelines shall be publicly accessible via a Public Dashboard.

54.8.8 Continental Execution Capability

The Development Assembly system shall be capable of coordinated, parallel execution across multiple regions and countries.

Through delegated authority under the PAN-Ambassador and Executives, assemblies may be conducted simultaneously, enabling continent-scale development alignment within compressed timeframes.

54.8.9 Principle of Accelerated Development

The system shall operate under:

Citizen-prioritized development (CDE projects)

Government collaboration (non-voted alignment)

Parallel execution

Strict timelines

Full transparency

No development project funded through the Foundation shall exist outside this system.

54.8.10 Consequences for Repeated Failure

(a) If a government entity fails to meet agreed timelines in two consecutive Concords, the Foundation may:

    (i) Redirect its share of the Country Budget Pot to CDE-led execution for the affected projects;

    (ii) Require government personnel to undergo efficiency training before future funding is released.

(b) If a CDE fails to meet agreed timelines in two consecutive Concords, the PAN-Ambassador may:

    (i) Recommend restructuring of CDE leadership;

(ii) Reallocate future CDE funding to higher-performing CDEs in the region.

54.8.11 Principle of Development Neutrality and Protection

(a) The Development Assembly system is a development coordination mechanism, not a political institution.

(b) Government officials participate as partners in development, contributing plans, resources, and execution capacity, without subordination to the Foundation.

(c) The Foundation shall remain strictly non-political and shall not:(i) Endorse or oppose any political party, candidate, or movement;(ii) Fund, organize, or support political activities, protests, or strikes;(iii) Interfere in the internal political affairs of any state.

(d) Development operations shall continue irrespective of:(i) Changes in government;(ii) Political transitions or instability;(iii) Strikes or protests not directly affecting operational safety.

(e) In the event of a strike or protest:(i) Operations shall be temporarily paused only where safety risks are present;(ii) The Foundation shall not take sides or engage in political positions;(iii) Work shall resume immediately once conditions are deemed safe.

(f) Principle of Development NeutralityAll development projects under the CDE framework shall be considered neutral public goods, serving communities irrespective of political affiliation, ideology, or leadership.

(g) Community Responsibility for ProtectionCDE projects are people-centered initiatives. Communities benefiting from such projects are encouraged to uphold their protection and continuity, including during periods of unrest, in order to prevent disruption, delays, or loss of future development opportunities.

(h) Continuity SafeguardWhere repeated disruption, destruction, or insecurity significantly affects project delivery within a region, future project prioritization and resource allocation may be reassessed to ensure responsible and sustainable deployment of funds

54.11 Enforcement & severability

54.11.1 Any breach of this Article shall be treated as a violation of Ethics, Anti-Corruption & Sanctions (Article XXII) and Anti-Diversion of Foundation Assets (Article XXVI), triggering immediate investigation by the Oversight Panel and potential sanctions including removal and restitution.

54.11.2 This Article is designated as a Tier 2 Core Article under Article XIX.3, requiring supermajority approvals for amendments to prevent weakening of continental safeguards.

54.11.3 If any provision is found invalid, the Severability clause (Article XXVI.0) shall apply, preserving the ADN's core structure and mission alignment.

 54.12 Incorporation of Private Annex L

The provisions of confidential ANNEX L (PAN-Ambassador Selection, Election Mechanics, Term Renewal, and Vacancy Procedures) are incorporated by reference and have binding legal effect on the Foundation, both Trusts, the Independent Auditor General, and all PAN-Ambassador candidates and appointees. Any dispute regarding the interpretation or application of ANNEX L shall be resolved in accordance with Article XVIII (Dispute Resolution).

Article LV - Incorporation and Binding Effect of Private Constitution Annexes

55.1 Legal Status of Annexes

The confidential ANNEXES of the Private Constitution are incorporated by reference into this public Constitution. These ANNEXES contain detailed operational, procedural, security, and succession mechanisms that are binding on all Foundation organs, trustees, officers, employees, volunteers, and affiliates.

55.2 Hierarchy of Instruments

a) In the event of any direct and irreconcilable conflict between a provision of this public Constitution and a provision of any ANNEX, the public Constitution shall prevail.

b) The ANNEXES shall be interpreted in a manner consistent with the public Constitution to the greatest extent possible.

c) The ANNEXES supplement, but do not replace or override, the substantive protections of the Core Articles.

55.3 Binding Effect

All persons subject to this Constitution, including but not limited to the Steward President, Trustees of both Trusts, Board members, IAG, PAN-Ambassadors, Executive Directors, employees, and volunteers, are bound by the provisions of the confidential ANNEXES as if those provisions were fully set forth in this public Constitution.

55.4 Access and Confidentiality

No ANNEX may be publicly disclosed if such disclosure would compromise security protocols, vetting methodologies, identity protection measures, or emergency response capabilities.

55.5 Amendment of Annexes

a) Amendments to any ANNEX that do not conflict with the public Constitution may be made by unanimous resolution of the Combined Trusts Board, with written certification from the IAG that the amendment does not violate any Core Article.

b) Any amendment to an ANNEX that would alter a substantive right or protection enumerated in a Core Article shall require compliance with the amendment procedures of Article XIX applicable to that Core Article.

c) All ANNEX amendments shall be recorded and maintained by the Secretary-General, with notice provided to all persons bound thereby

55.6 Dispute Resolution

Any dispute regarding the interpretation, application, or enforceability of any ANNEX provision shall be resolved in accordance with Article XVIII (Dispute Resolution). The confidential nature of the ANNEXES shall be preserved in any arbitration or legal proceeding to the maximum extent permitted by law.

55.7 Core Article Status

This Article is designated as a Tier 1 Core Article under Article XIX.3 and may be amended only through the procedures therein.

ARTICLE LVI - MISSION-ALIGNED BOND INSTRUMENTS

56.1 Principle of Citizen Partnership

The Foundation shall not take loans, debt, or credit facilities from banks, financial institutions, or any corporate entity. The Foundation shall not issue commercial paper, enter credit agreements, or incur any form of institutional debt.

The only permitted form of borrowing is the issuance of bonds to the public under the Mission Investment Agreement - African citizens, diaspora communities, global citizens and mission-aligned institutional investors - as a means of citizen partnership in the Foundation's growth.

56.2 Permitted Bond Classes

The Foundation may issue only two classes of bonds under The GREAT AFRICAN GRID BOND:

(a) Digital Ecosystem Bond – to fund technology development, venture capital programs, research and innovation, and digital infrastructure.

Infrastructure Bond – to fund physical infrastructure projects

No other bond classes or debt instruments of any kind are permitted.

56.3 Per-Investor Limit

No single investor (whether individual, corporation, organization, or institution) may purchase more than $1 million USD(or local currency equivalent) of bonds in total across all series and all bond classes.

This limit mirrors the Tier 2 donation cap in Article XXXIV and serves the same purpose: preventing any single investor from gaining undue influence over the Foundation.

56.4 No Per-Series Total Limit

The total amount raised in any bond series is not limited by this Article. The Foundation may raise $1M, $100M, $1B, or more in a single series, subject to market conditions and Combined Trusts Board approval. Only the per-investor cap applies.

56.5 Oversight

No subsequent bond after the first Grid bond set by the founder may be issued without:

(a) Unanimous approval of the Combined Trusts;

(b) Certification by the IAG that the proceeds will be used for mission-aligned purposes;

(c) Confirmation that the per-investor limit is strictly enforced.

56.6 Use of Proceeds

Bond proceeds shall be held in ring-fenced accounts and disbursed only for the purposes stated in the bond prospectus. Proceeds may not be used for staff salaries, or administrative costs.

56.7 Transparency

All bond issuances shall be disclosed on the Public Dashboard, including:

(a) Bond class and series number;

(b) Total amount raised;

(c) Number of investors (aggregated, not individual identities);

(d) Use of proceeds;

(e) Repayment status.

56.8 No Private Inurement

No bondholder acquires any equity, voting rights, governance authority, or ownership interest in the Foundation, its Trusts, or its Operating Companies by virtue of holding a bond. Bonds confer no influence whatsoever over Foundation decisions.

56.9 Repayment Sources

Bonds shall be repaid from any lawful source of Foundation revenue, including but not limited to:

(a) Revenue generated by the projects or infrastructure the bonds funded;

(b) Revenue from other Foundation projects, operating companies, or investments;

(c) Tier 1 and Tier 2 donations;

(d) Any other mission-aligned revenue stream.

The Foundation shall prioritize project-specific revenue for repayment but may use other sources as needed. Donations are a permitted backup, consistent with Article XXXIV.

56.10 Application to New Dawn City

(a) The bonds authorized under this Article may also be issued specifically for the development, construction, and expansion of New Dawn City(Article LVII.2(c)).

(b) Proceeds from Infrastructure Bonds issued for New Dawn City shall be allocated to:

    (i) Core infrastructure ;

    (ii) Zone agreement costs;

    (iii) Replication toolkit development;

    (iv) Such other purposes as the Combined Trusts Board may designate,consistent with the New Dawn City mandate.

(c) Proceeds from Digital Ecosystem Bonds issued for New Dawn City shall be allocated to:

    (i) Smart city technology deployment;

    (ii) Jowih Credits infrastructure;

    (iii) Technology integration from  R&D Centers.

(d) All bond proceeds for New Dawn City are subject to the Asset Lock (Article XXV) and may not be diverted to any other purpose without unanimous Combined Trusts Board approval and IAG certification.

56.11 Core Article Status

This Article is designated as a Tier 2 Core Article under Article XIX.3. No amendment may:

(a) Add new bond classes beyond the two permitted;

(b) Increase the per-investor limit above $1 million;

(c) Permit the Foundation to take institutional debt of any kind.

ARTICLE LVII - THE JOWIH CITADEL AND HEADQUARTERS FRAMEWORK

Recital

The Foundation serves all fifty-four African nations equally. To fulfil its Mission effectively, the Foundation requires a permanent headquarters that is functionally neutral and insulated from the domestic politics of any single host State.

Accordingly, the Foundation shall establish the Jowih Citadel as a Tier 1 Neutral Zone on African soil under a Headquarters Agreement and such other legal instruments as may be required under applicable law and international practice.

The Foundation does not seek to diminish host State sovereignty. It seeks functional neutrality, security, and continuity for its core Mission. The host State that agrees to host the Jowih Citadel shall be recognised as the Guardian of Africa’s Neutral Headquarters.

New Dawn City and any other physical presence of the Foundation shall be established through separate bilateral agreements, treaties, frameworks, or project-specific instruments as appropriate.

57.1 Authority to Establish the Citadel

57.1.1 The Founder and the Combined Trusts Board are authorised to negotiate, approve, and conclude Headquarters Agreements, host State implementing legislation where required, and any ancillary instruments necessary for the establishment and operation of the Jowih Citadel as a Tier 1 Neutral Zone.

57.1.2 The Foundation may establish additional neutral or specially protected zones in other host States where necessary for operational redundancy, political neutrality, security, or Mission continuity, subject to separate agreements.

57.1.3 All such agreements shall be based on good faith, mutual benefit, respect for host State sovereignty, and protection of the Foundation’s constitutional independence.

57.2 The Jowih Citadel - Permanent Headquarters

57.2.1 The Jowih Citadel shall be the permanent seat, registered office, and principal place of governance of the Foundation. Upon establishment of the Citadel, any temporary registered office, including any interim foreign registration arrangement, shall be replaced in accordance with the applicable legal transition documents.

57.2.2 The Citadel, including its designated premises and appurtenant grounds, shall enjoy functional inviolability to the fullest extent permitted by the Headquarters Agreement and applicable law. Entry by host State authorities shall require the prior written consent of the Steward President, except in circumstances expressly provided for in the Headquarters Agreement or required by applicable law.

57.2.3 The Citadel shall be subject to the Asset Lock under Article XXV and may not be sold, transferred, encumbered, expropriated, or repurposed in any manner inconsistent with the Mission or the Constitution.

57.2.4 The Foundation Constitution shall apply fully within the Citadel in respect of internal governance, the Asset Lock, intellectual property, operational continuity, and Mission-related functions. Host State law shall apply only to those matters expressly reserved to it under the Headquarters Agreement or otherwise required by applicable law.

57.3 Other Physical Presences - Separate Agreements and Frameworks

The Foundation may establish other physical presences, including but not limited to New Dawn City, regional offices, R&D Centres, Special Economic Zones, project sites, and related facilities, through separate bilateral agreements, treaties, frameworks, or project-specific instruments.

These may include, without limitation:

(a) Host Government Agreements;(b) Special Economic Zone agreements;(c) Headquarters Agreements for regional offices;(d) Project-specific implementation agreements;(e) Stabilisation and expropriation protection clauses;(f) Data sovereignty and intellectual property frameworks;(g) Tax, customs, and fiscal treatment agreements;(h) Dispute resolution protocols providing for binding international arbitration;(i) Any other lawful arrangement necessary for Mission delivery.

All such arrangements shall respect host State sovereignty while protecting the Foundation’s Mission, assets, personnel, data, and operational independence.

57.4 Core Protections

The Foundation shall seek to include the following protections, as appropriate, in all relevant agreements:

(a) Stability of the legal, regulatory, fiscal, and administrative framework applicable to the relevant project or zone;(b) Protection against expropriation, nationalisation, or arbitrary deprivation;(c) Data sovereignty and confidentiality protections;(d) Asset Lock protections for Foundation-owned or Foundation-controlled core infrastructure;(e) Binding international arbitration for disputes;(f) Tax and customs treatment no less favourable than comparable lawful arrangements;(g) Expedited entry, residence, and movement arrangements for Foundation personnel where necessary for Mission delivery.

57.5 Host State Benefits

The host State of the Jowih Citadel shall receive:

The prestige of being recognised as the Guardian of Africa’s Neutral Headquarters;

GDP boost from the advanced city of the future economy called The New Dawn City(TND~City) and 10% of total revenue from the City.The right to host continental events and diplomatic gatherings associated with the Foundation’s Mission;

57.6 Core Article Status

This Article LVII is designated a Tier 1 Core Article of the Constitution. No amendment shall eliminate:

(a) the requirement for a Tier 1 Neutral Zone for the Jowih Citadel on African soil;(b) the functional inviolability and permanent status of the Jowih Citadel as the Foundation’s principal headquarters;(c) the Asset Lock protection applying to core infrastructure governed by this Article.

Any amendment purporting to do any of the foregoing shall be void ab initio within the Foundation’s constitutional order.

ARTICLE LVIII - MISSION-THREATENING ACTION AND INSTITUTIONAL PROTECTION

58.0 Status and Supremacy

(a) This Article is designated a Tier 1 Core Article of the Constitution.

(b) It shall be afforded the highest level of protection, enforcement priority, and interpretive weight within the internal governance framework of the Foundation.

(c) No amendment, suspension, or reinterpretation of this Article shall be permitted except in accordance with Tier 1 amendment procedures under Article XIX.3.

58.1 Nature of the Offense

(a) A Mission-Threatening Action constitutes the gravest internal breach of duty against the Foundation.

(b) It shall be understood as any intentional or grossly negligent act or omission that directly or indirectly undermines, subverts, or places at severe risk the Mission, the Foundation's institutional integrity, or the long-term interests of the beneficiaries the Foundation serves.

(c) Any Mission-Threatening Action shall be treated internally as a direct attack on:

(i) The Mission as defined in Article II;

(ii) The institutional integrity and constitutional order of the Foundation;

(iii) The safety, authority, and continuity of the Steward President, the Foundation CEO, and the Independent Auditor General (IAG);

(iv) The stability and security of all operational, strategic, and protected zones;

(v) The assets, intellectual property, and infrastructure of the Foundation.

58.2 Definition of Mission-Threatening Action

(a) A Mission-Threatening Action shall mean any act or omission, direct or indirect, that intentionally or recklessly undermines, infiltrates, subverts, captures, or places at risk the Mission, its leadership, its assets, or its long-term objectives.

(b) This includes, but is not limited to:

(i) Strategic sabotage of Foundation operations, assets, or projects;

(ii) Corruption, misappropriation, or unauthorized diversion of substantial resources, including amounts exceeding $1,000,000 USD (or local currency equivalent);

(iii) External influence, capture, or coercion exercised over Foundation personnel, decision-making, or assets;

(iv) Concealed control, direction, or puppetry by any external actor;

(v) Concealment of material threats to the Foundation's Mission or operations;

(vi) Unauthorized disclosure, misuse, or weaponization of proprietary or sensitive information;

(vii) Exposure of leadership or core operational structures to harm, compromise, or unlawful access;

(viii) Any act constituting infiltration, capture, or unauthorized third-party control as defined in this Article.

(c) "Recklessly or intentionally" shall be established through clear and convincing evidence gathered during an investigation.

58.3 Infiltration, Capture, and Misdirection

(a) Joining or operating within the Foundation on behalf of, or under the unlawful direction of, a hostile or misaligned external entity constitutes a Mission-Threatening Action.

(b) Infiltration is deemed complete upon entry with such intent, regardless of whether a subsequent harmful act is fully executed.

(c) Unauthorized external control includes any situation where operational independence is nominal but direction, influence, or control is exerted by an undisclosed external actor.

58.4 Misprision and Reporting Obligations

(a) Failure by any officer, trustee, or senior employee to report a known or reasonably suspected Mission-Threatening Action constitutes a serious breach of fiduciary and constitutional duty.

(b) Any person subject to this Constitution who knowingly conceals, fails to report, or actively suppresses information regarding a Mission-Threatening Action shall be subject to disciplinary action and potential joint liability to the fullest extent permitted by applicable contract and host-state law.

58.5 Investigation and Determination

58.5A Independent Ad Hoc Investigative Panel

(a) All internal investigations of suspected Mission-Threatening Actions shall be conducted by an Independent Ad Hoc Investigative Panel acting as an extraordinary fact-finding body.

(b) The Panel shall:

(i) Be composed of independent experts with verified integrity, legal or forensic competence, and objectivity;

(ii) Be structurally insulated from internal management and executive influence;

(iii) Be constituted on a case-by-case basis and dissolved upon submission of its final report.

(c) The Panel shall coordinate with the Independent Auditor General (IAG) except where the IAG is implicated or reasonably suspected of conflict or compromise.

(d) The Panel shall have internal authority to:

(i) Access all Foundation records, physical facilities, electronic systems, and communications within the Foundation’s legal control;

(ii) Require written statements and cooperation from all persons bound by Foundation contracts and governance policies;

(iii) Recommend the immediate temporary suspension of operational duties or system access pending investigation;

(iv) Direct Foundation legal counsel to apply to competent courts of host jurisdictions for urgent interim relief, including Mareva injunctions or asset-freezing orders, where lawful and necessary;

(v) Refer evidence, upon authorization by the Board, to host-state law enforcement or statutory regulatory bodies.

58.5B Non-Self Investigation Principle

No individual, body, or executive structure shall investigate itself or participate in panel deliberations regarding its own conduct.

58.5C Investigation Integrity

Any attempt by any officer or employee to influence, obstruct, delay, or interfere with an investigation under this Article constitutes a separate Mission-Threatening Action and grounds for summary contractual termination.

58.5D Determination

Determination of a Mission-Threatening Action shall be made by the Combined Trusts Board by not less than a three-quarters (3/4) supermajority vote of un-conflicted members present and voting.

58.5E Selection and Vetting of the Independent Panel

(a) Panel members shall be selected from a pre-vetted registry of external legal, financial, and investigative professionals.

(b) Candidates shall undergo mandatory conflict-of-interest screening prior to appointment.

(c) Panel proceedings and evidence shall be protected under strict confidentiality protocols, subject to legal disclosure requirements under applicable law.

58.6 Governance Board Participation and Attendance

(a) All non-conflicted members of the Combined Trusts Board are required to participate in deliberations and voting under this Article.

(b) Absence is permitted only in cases of verified incapacity, force majeure, or formal conflict of interest.

(c) Coordinated absence or deliberate refusal to participate intended to defeat a quorum or obstruct proceedings shall constitute a breach of governance duty and trigger an independent review under this Article.

58.7 Internal Sanctions, Civil Recovery, and Legal Jurisdiction

(a) Any person determined by the Combined Trusts Board to have committed a Mission-Threatening Action shall face internal and civil sanctions to the maximum extent permitted by applicable contract and host-state employment or corporate law, including:

(i) Summary termination of employment, directorship, or official status with the Foundation;

(ii) Permanent disqualification from future association with the Foundation or its affiliates;

(iii) Enforcement of contractual clawback provisions regarding unvested benefits, discretionary bonuses, or incentives;

(iv) Civil litigation for full restitution of diverted assets and damages;

(v) Formal referral of the investigative findings to competent statutory law enforcement agencies and prosecutorial authorities.

(b) Jurisdiction and Dispute Resolution:

(i) All civil governance disputes, asset recovery claims, or contractual breaches arising under this Article shall be referred to binding International Commercial Arbitration under recognized arbitration rules (such as LCIA, KIAC, or AFSA) as specified in the applicable employment agreement or trust deed.

(ii) Criminal acts, financial crimes, or breaches of local statutory law shall be referred to the competent law enforcement agencies and courts of the host state where the offense occurred, or any jurisdiction possessing lawful statutory jurisdiction over the offense or the offender.

58.8 Strategic Cooperation Exception

(a) The Combined Trusts Board may, upon recommendation of the Independent Panel and by a 90% supermajority vote, enter into a settlement or cooperation agreement with a responding party.

(b) Such agreement may reduce internal civil claims or alter administrative sanctions in exchange for full disclosure, asset recovery assistance, or evidence uncovering broader institutional compromise, provided such agreement does not violate local criminal or statutory reporting laws.

58.9 Whistleblower Safeguards and Incentives

(a) Good-faith reporting of suspected Mission-Threatening Actions shall be strictly protected under the Foundation’s Whistleblower & Ethics Policy.

(b) Secure, confidential reporting channels shall be maintained by the IAG or the Independent Panel.

(c) The Board may authorize a discretionary financial reward, consistent with local tax and financial regulations, for reports that lead directly to the successful recovery of stolen or misappropriated assets.

(d) Knowingly false, malicious, or bad-faith accusations shall constitute grounds for internal disciplinary action and legal liability.

58.10 Statute of Limitations

To the fullest extent permitted by applicable civil and criminal law, internal governance proceedings, civil asset recovery claims, and formal law enforcement referrals under this Article shall not be subject to internal time limitations.

58.11 Emergency Defensive Measures

(a) Where a Mission-Threatening Action presents an ongoing or imminent threat to physical safety, financial security, or critical IT infrastructure, the Steward President, CEO, or IAG may take immediate precautionary measures.

(b) Emergency measures are strictly administrative and may include:

(i) Temporary administrative suspension of access to systems, bank accounts, and facilities;

(ii) Precautionary suspension of operational duties pending panel review.

(c) All emergency measures must be formally documented and submitted to the Independent Panel and Board within fourteen (14) days for review and ratification.

58.12 Severability and Compliance with Local Law

If any provision of this Article is found by a court or arbitral tribunal of competent jurisdiction to be invalid, illegal, or unenforceable under applicable host-state law, such provision shall be severed or modified to the minimum extent necessary to render it enforceable, and the remaining provisions of this Article shall remain in full force and effect.

58.13 Core Article Status

This Article is designated as a Tier 1 Core Article under Article XIX.3 and may be amended only through the procedures therein.

ARTICLE LIX - JOWIH CREDITS

Recital

Jowih Credits are established as a stable, asset-backed digital utility bridge token. They are not a speculative cryptocurrency. They do not involve mining, staking, interest, yield, or dividends.

Across Africa, cross-border trade and remittances are constrained by limited access to major reserve currencies. This constraint creates friction like delays, high costs, and reduced economic activity , not because of any failure in the global financial system, but because of the structural reality of currency availability.

Jowih Credits are designed to provide a neutral, independently governed bridge infrastructure through which participating states, financial institutions, and approved payment providers may facilitate cross-border transactions on an equitable and interoperable basis.

The system is intended to support greater connectivity between national currencies and payment systems while respecting the monetary, financial, and institutional frameworks of each participating state. National currencies remain central to domestic economic activity, while the JOWIH Credit infrastructure provides an additional bridge for eligible cross-border transactions.

Through Credit-Routed FX and interoperable payment infrastructure, the system seeks to improve the efficiency and circulation of available currency liquidity across participating markets, facilitate trade and payments, and reduce unnecessary friction arising from fragmented currency and payment systems.

Participation is founded upon cooperation, mutual benefit, equal treatment, and respect for the sovereignty and financial systems of participating states.

The Foundation's primary economic model is the Treasury Engine ; holding Credits as a stable, asset-backed store of value. The Foundation does not earn from transaction fees. Instead, it earns from the float generated by held Credits, which are invested in mission-aligned projects with all returns flowing to the Mission Fund. This positions the Foundation as a neutral infrastructure layer, not a competitor to partners.

Jowih Credits operate through a partner-only execution model. The Foundation issues and holds the credit. Partners handle ALL conversions. The Foundation does not operate any exchange, conversion, or execution service. This ensures the Foundation never competes with partners and remains purely the credit layer.

Credits exist solely in the bridge layer. They never enter the domestic economy. When Credits are used for domestic spending, partners convert them instantly to local currency in the background. This conversion is automatic, seamless, and invisible to the user. Partners handle the conversion at the point of spending.

Auto-Conversion is the default mode for all transactions. Holding Credits is optional. Credits are a bridge, not a currency.

Jowih Credits shall be issued and operated in compliance with the Mauritius VAITOS Act 2021, FATF Recommendation 15, and applicable host country laws. The Foundation shall obtain and maintain Class S, Class O, Class M, and Class R licenses from the Mauritius Financial Services Commission, or such consolidated license classes as the FSC may permit.

Jowih Credits are designed to accelerate the African Continental Free Trade Area by providing a stable, low-cost, instant settlement layer for intra-African trade. They complement, rather than compete with, existing foreign exchange markets and payment systems.

59.1 Definitions

(a) "Jowih Credit" means the Foundation-issued, asset-backed digital utility bridge token.

(b) Jowih Credits are not legal tender, not a currency, and not a security under the Mauritius Securities Act 2005.

(c) Jowih Credits confer no ownership, voting, profit, interest, yield, or investment rights in the Foundation or any affiliated entity.

(d) "Jowih Ecosystem" includes New Dawn City, the Jowih COS, Foundation-owned assets and projects, spin-off companies, and approved partners.

(e) "PSPS" means Payment Service Provider Systems, including mobile money operators (e.g., M-Pesa, MTN, Airtel), banks, fintech payment gateways (e.g., Flutterwave, Paystack), card networks (Mastercard, Visa), global fintech platforms (PayPal, Stripe), and other licensed payment processors the Foundation may partner with.

(f) "High-Liquid Reserves" means cash, gold bullion, short-term government securities, and such other highly liquid assets as the Combined Trusts Board may designate, with a target allocation of not less than thirty percent (30%) and not more than fifty percent (50%) of total backing.

(g) "Foundation Assets" means current and future revenue streams from the Ecosystem, Foundation-owned companies, real estate off Zone and within Zones, ADFA projects, and any other high-value assets approved by the Combined Trusts Board, with a target allocation of not less than fifty percent (50%) and not more than seventy percent (70%) of total backing.

Provided that High-Liquid Reserves and Foundation Assets shall at all times sum to one hundred percent (100%) of total backing.

(h) "Partner Channels" means end user channels operated by PSPSs, banks, card networks, and fintech platforms for individuals, SMEs, governments, corporations, and institutional investors.

(i) "Flow Engine" means the Foundation's transaction layer where partners handle high-frequency cross-border flows (remittances, SME trade, platform payments) and earn their own margins.

(j) "Treasury Engine" means the Foundation's value-holding layer where Credits are held in the Foundation Treasury, allowing governments, institutions, and multiplayers to hold value because it is stable and asset-backed. The Foundation earns from the float generated by held Credits.

(k) "Auto-Conversion Mode" means the default mode where Credits convert instantly at FX layer from one currency to another local currency or internal from credit to local currency for domestic spending, and to preferred currency for cross-border receipt. This is the system's default behavior.

(l) "Holding Mode" means the optional mode where users may choose to hold Jowih Credits in their wallets for future use.

59.2 Purpose

Jowih Credits shall function as:

(a) A stable bridge between African local currencies and foreign currencies, facilitating cross-border trade, remittances, and investment.

(b) A facilitator of low-cost, instant cross-border payments within Africa and globally, directly supporting the African Continental Free Trade Area (AfCFTA) by providing a reliable settlement layer for intra-African trade.

(c) A stable channel for global investors and diaspora to bring capital into Africa across all 54 countries easily increasing circulation of major currencies like USD, EUR, CNY to Africa.

(d) A complementary financial bridge infrastructure, that works alongside existing global and regional payment systems, offering an additional stable, fast, low-cost option for value transfer.

(e) A dual-engine model operating through partners who handle all conversions, while the Foundation focuses on the stable credit layer and treasury management, earning from the float generated by held Credits.

(f) The Foundation shall not earn from transaction fees. Its primary economic model is the Treasury Engine - holding Credits and investing the float in mission-aligned projects, with all returns flowing to the Mission Fund.

The Foundation shall actively promote the use of national currencies for all domestic obligations, salaries, stipends, and local transactions. Jowih Credits shall not replace or compete with national currencies for domestic use. Credits never enter the domestic economy. They convert instantly to local currency for domestic spending.

59.3 Regulatory Compliance

The Foundation shall:

(a) Obtain and maintain licenses from the Mauritius Financial Services Commission (FSC) under the VAITOS Act 2021 covering all its regulated activities, including:

(i) Class S (Virtual Asset Market Place) - License to operate the Foundation Exchange for matching orders and facilitating the exchange of Credits for fiat currency or other virtual assets;

(ii) Class O (Virtual Asset Wallet Services) - License to operate  Jowih wallets, enabling the transfer of Credits between users;

(iii) Class M (Virtual Asset Broker-Dealer) - License required because the Foundation facilitates the exchange between fiat currency and Jowih Credits;

(iv) Class R (Virtual Asset Custodian) - License required for the safekeeping and administration of client virtual assets and/or instruments enabling control over such virtual assets.

Provided that the Foundation may apply for consolidated or combined license classes if the FSC permits such treatment, reducing regulatory duplication while maintaining full compliance.

(b) Comply with FATF Recommendation 15 (new technologies and virtual assets) and all applicable AML/CFT requirements under the Financial Intelligence and Anti-Money Laundering Act (FIAMLA) 2002 (Mauritius) and the FSC's AML/CFT Handbook.

(c) Maintain a physical office in Mauritius and ensure that business activities are directed and managed from Mauritius, as a matter of substance.

(d) Never list Jowih Credits on any internal or external public cryptocurrency exchange for speculative trading.

(e) Designate a senior executive or law firm, residing in Mauritius, responsible for regulatory compliance.

(f) Cooperate with host country regulators where the Foundation operates exchange services, including obtaining equivalent licenses as required.

(g) Maintain the minimum required unimpaired capital for each license class.

(h) Segregate client funds from operational funds and maintain them in trust accounts as required by the FSC.

(i) File annual audited financial statements with the FSC.

59.4 Reserve Backing

Jowih Credits shall be 100% asset-backed. The reserve shall be held in trust, subject to the Asset Lock (Article XXV) and quarterly audited. The reserve composition shall be:

(a) High-Liquid Reserves (30-50% of total backing)

Consisting of:

(i) Cash in major currencies;

(ii) Gold bullion;

(iii) Short-term government securities;

(iv) Such other highly liquid assets as the Combined Trusts Board and Steward President may designate.

(b) Foundation Assets (50-70% of total backing)

Consisting of:

(i) Current and future revenue streams and services;

(ii) Foundation-owned companies, Data Centers, R&D, and spin-off companies;

(iii) Real estate and New Dawn City properties;

(iv) ADFA projects: Foundation-owned or Joint projects (receivables and future cash flows);

(v) Such other high-value assets as the Combined Trusts Board and Steward President may designate.

(c) Allocation Adjustment

The Combined Trusts Board through the company responsible for Jowih Credits may adjust the allocation within the ranges specified in (a) and (b) based on market conditions and risk assessment, provided that:

(i) High-Liquid Reserves shall never fall below 30% of total backing;

(ii) Foundation Assets shall never exceed 70% of total backing;

(iii) The reserve remains over-collateralized at a minimum ratio of 100% of Credits in circulation at all times.

(d) Audit and Transparency

The IAG Departments shall audit the reserve quarterly. Audit results shall be published on the Public Dashboard (Article XXXV) within 30 days. Annual auditing may be done by the External Auditor Firm.

59.5 Jowih COS Hybrid Wallet and PSPS Partnership & Integrations

(a) The Jowih Wallet shall partner with or integrate user PSPS accounts (mobile money, bank accounts, card networks, fintech wallets) into a single interface for respective countries to provide payment services in the Jowih COS and other PSPS-specific services to a broader user base. PSPSs shall retain full ownership of their customer relationships and data.

(b) For local transactions within a country in the Jowih COS:

(i) The default payment method shall be the user's local currency via their chosen PSPS mini-wallets;

(ii) The Foundation through partners shall not force Credits for domestic transactions.

(iii) When a user selects pay with Credits for domestic spending, Credits convert instantly to local currency in the background through the partner.

(c) For cross-border and global transactions:

(i) The preferred payment method in the Jowih COS shall be Jowih Credits to facilitate faster cross-border payments;

(ii) Users may choose alternative local and international currencies too directly.

(d) The Foundation shall partner with PSPSs, banks, card networks, and fintech platforms to enable:

(i) Users to convert and spend Jowih Credits on bills, merchants, and external apps through existing partner infrastructure;

(ii) PSPSs, banks, card networks, and fintech platforms to integrate Jowih Credits Wallet APIs and earn fees, creating new additional revenue streams and settlements;

(iii) Partners handle the conversion of Credits to local currency at the point of domestic spending. This is their core role in the ecosystem.

(e) The Foundation's Jowih COS company shall never compete with existing or future PSPSs, banks, card networks, or fintech platforms. The Foundation shall partner with them, driving new customers and new revenue to PSPSs, banks, card networks, and fintech platforms while extending Credit utility. This does not limit the Foundation from owning a bank.

Therefore, the Jowih wallet shall never be redesigned in a way to do away with PSPSs, banks, card networks, and fintech platforms handling respective wallets they connect to the Jowih COS for user selections as primary wallets, as they will remain the default face of the Jowih Wallet and the backbone for transactions.

(f) The Foundation shall not operate any fiat on-ramp or off-ramp services in any market. The Foundation's role is limited to the bridge layer (issuance, holding, and transfer of Credits). All conversions - retail and wholesale - are handled exclusively by partners. Partners with capacity may handle wholesale conversions. Partners may also route flows to Tier 1 institutions at their discretion. This provision ensures partners remain the default face of the Jowih Wallet and the backbone for all conversions.

59.6 Operational Model

(a) The Foundation shall pay all salaries, stipends, and local obligations in the respective national currencies to boost local currency circulation.

(b) The Foundation may pay contracts, ADFA disbursements, government partnerships, and investments in Jowih Credits.

(c) Inside New Dawn City, residents and businesses may use local currency, but Credits are presumed for efficiency and stability. For domestic spending, Credits convert instantly to local currency in the background through partners.

(d) Outside the Jowih Ecosystem, individuals, governments, and companies may voluntarily convert national currency into Jowih Credits via Partner Channels.

(e) Dual-Mode Operation:

(i) Auto-Conversion Mode (Default): This is the default mode for all transactions. When a user sends funds from one currency to another, the system automatically converts to Jowih Credits for transfer and then to the recipient's chosen currency upon receipt. For domestic spending, Credits convert instantly to local currency in the background. This mode is designed for instant settlement, cross-border trade, remittances, bill payments, and everyday spending involving different currencies. Credits are minted and burned in real time. Reserve requirements are minimal.

(ii) Holding Mode (Optional): Users may choose to hold Jowih Credits in their wallets temporarily for six months. Credits are fully asset-backed and may be used for future payments, savings, or strategic purposes. The Foundation may utilize the float generated by held Credits for strategic investments, with all returns flowing to the Mission Fund or Treasury. Holding is optional, users are not required to hold Credits.

(f) User Choice and Flexibility:

(i) Auto-Conversion is the default mode for all users.

(ii) Holding Mode is optional - users may choose to hold Credits, but are not required to.

(iii) Users may switch between modes at any time.

(iv) No user is compelled to hold Credits.

(v) For domestic spending, Credits always convert instantly to local currency in the background, regardless of mode.

(g) Government and Partner Integration: The Foundation shall partner with PSPSs, banks, card networks, fintech platforms, and governments to enable seamless conversion between local currencies and Credits. Governments receive their tax share on all on-ramp and off-ramp transactions. PSPSs, banks, and fintech platforms earn fees on conversions and local transactions.

59.7 Fees, Government Tax Share, and Market Structure

The government tax share (both foreign governments overseas and local governments in Africa) recognizes that on-ramp and off-ramp transactions occur within each state's sovereign jurisdiction and contributes to the public revenue pool that funds development - a goal shared with the Foundation.

(a) Market Structure - Partner-Only Execution

Jowih Credits operates through a partner-only execution model:

(i) The Foundation does not operate any exchange or conversion service. Partners handle ALL conversions - retail and wholesale.

(ii) Wholesale Channel: Serves governments (treasuries), commercial banks, corporations, airports, airlines, institutional investors, and large partners with capacity. Partners set their own pricing based on the Foundation base rates.

(iii) Retail Channel: Serves individuals, SMEs, and everyday users through PSPSs (mobile money, banks, card networks, fintech platforms). Partners apply their margin on top of the Foundation base rates. There is no minimum transaction.

(iv) Partners as executioners do not hold Jowih Credits. They are conversion rails only. Their role is to convert Credits to local currency for domestic spending. But they can hold credits as clients in their partner wallet for their treasuries, investment and any other needs they have.

(v) Credits never enter the domestic economy. They are a bridge layer only. For domestic spending, Credits convert instantly to local currency through partners.

(b) Foundation Base Rates

(i) The Foundation does not charge any transaction fee on conversions. Partners set their own pricing based on the government tax base and their own margins.

(ii) The base rates for conversions are as follows:

(A) For wallet-to-wallet Jowih Credit transfers: 0% to 1% partner transaction fee. No government tax, as there is no realization event.

(B) For on-ramp and off-ramp conversions: 0% Foundation fee and 0.25% to 0.30% government tax, totaling 0.25% to 0.30% base.

(C) For auto-conversions involving local transaction using pay with credits method directly: 0% Foundation fee, 0.1% to 1% partner fee, and 0.1% to 0.30% government tax.

(iii) The base rates shall be published in the Operational Framework and may be adjusted by the Combined Trusts Board in accordance with the amendment requirements of this Article.

(c) Partner Margins

(i) Partners (PSPSs, banks, card networks, fintech platforms) may add a margin on top of the government tax base for conversions handled through their channels.

(ii) The maximum partner margin shall be 1.5% for on-ramp/off-ramp conversions and 0.1% to 1% for local transactions.

(iii) Partners may compete on margins. Partners earn the margin they add. Partners may decide to set tiers on transaction and apply different margins as per the market condition.

(d) Government Tax Remittance

(i) The government tax of 0.10%-0.30% on local transaction from credits direct or 0.25% to 0.30% on ramps is applied to the base rate.

(ii) The tax is withheld at the point of transaction by the partner.

(iii) The Foundation remits all government taxes to respective governments monthly, or immediately where infrastructure exists.

(iv) Partners are not responsible for tax remittance. The Foundation handles it.

(v) Governments receive auditable reports of all taxable transactions within their jurisdiction.

(e) Foundation Revenue Model

(i) The Foundation does not earn from transaction fees.

(ii) The Foundation's primary economic model is the Treasury Engine - holding Credits and investing the float generated by held Credits in mission-aligned projects.

(iii) All returns from float investments flow to the Mission Fund.

59.8 Holding and Conversion

(a) Jowih Credits earn no interest, yield, or dividends.

(b) Who Holds Credits:

(i) Jowih Credits are held in the Foundation Treasury as reserve backing and liquidity provision;

(ii) Individual users may hold Credits in their Jowih credit wallets for future use maximum six months;

(iii) Partners, Institutions, governments, and ADFA contractors may hold Credits too with agreements in case of extension;

(iv) Partners (PSPSs, banks, card networks, fintech platforms) connect to user's Jowih wallet through APIs. They are conversion rails only. Their role is to convert Credits to local currency for domestic spending.

(v) Holding Credits is optional. Auto-Conversion is the default.

(c) Recipients of Jowih Credits may hold them for a maximum of six (6) months. After six months, the holder is mandated to convert to local currency for domestic needs, with a grace period of one week for anyone who may have forgotten to do so.

(d) Premium Wallets dedicated between the Foundation and hospitals or Foundation and Country Pots under the AHF framework shall have a time limit of seven (7) days after the funds have been received.

(e) Conversion to local or foreign currency shall be available almost instantly through the Jowih Credits blockchain wallet in Partner Channels.

(f) The Foundation may extend the holding period for specific categories of holders (e.g., governments, commercial banks, institutional investors, ADFA contractors) by agreement, subject to anti-speculation safeguards.

(g) Jowih Credits are not an investment vehicle. The Foundation shall not market them as such.

59.9 AfCFTA and Continental Trade

Jowih Credits are specifically designed to accelerate the African Continental Free Trade Area (AfCFTA) by:

(a) Providing a stable, low-cost, instant settlement layer for intra-African trade.

(b) Enabling small and medium-sized enterprises (SMEs) to participate in cross-border trade much easier.

(c) Reducing the cost of cross-border payments and remittances from current levels to under 3%.

(d) Complementing the AfCFTA Payment and Settlement System by providing a digital bridge between the 54 national currencies of AfCFTA member states.

(e) Supporting the AU's Agenda 2063 goal of a fully integrated, prosperous, and peaceful Africa.

59.10 Data Sovereignty and Protection

(a) Cross-border settlement data shall be anonymized and aggregated.

(b) The Foundation shall not monetize or share individual transaction data without explicit user consent.

(c) Partnered PSPSs, banks, card networks, and fintech platforms retain full ownership of their customer data.

59.11 Credit Valuation Framework

59.11.1 Valuation Principle

Jowih Credits ("Credits") shall operate under a structured valuation framework designed to ensure:

- Stability in everyday use

- Global interoperability

- Long-term monetary resilience

The valuation framework shall distinguish between:

- Operational pricing reference, and

- Underlying reserve backing

59.11.2 Primary Reference (Normal Conditions)

Under normal market conditions, Credits shall be valued using a United States Dollar (USD) reference:

1 Jowih Credit = 1 USD equivalent (for pricing and transactional purposes).

This reference shall:

- Serve as a unit of account only

- Facilitate ease of trade, settlement, and adoption

- Not constitute a dependency on USD for underlying value

59.11.3 Independence from Currency Anchors

Notwithstanding the USD reference:

- Credits shall remain fully asset-backed in accordance with Article 59.4

- The reserve shall be diversified across currencies, commodities, and real assets

- No single currency shall define or limit the intrinsic value of Credits

59.11.4 Contingency Valuation Framework (Re-anchoring Mechanism)

To ensure continuity under conditions of monetary instability, the system shall incorporate a Contingency Valuation Framework.

Under qualifying conditions, the valuation of Credits may transition from the USD reference to a Basket Reference Unit (BRU).

59.11.5 Basket Reference Unit (BRU)

The Basket Reference Unit shall represent a diversified valuation standard, which may include but is not limited to:

(a) Percentages portion in diversified major currencies

(b) Percentage portion in Gold or equivalent precious metals

(c) Percentage portion in Asset Index, representing real-economy assets and productive value

The composition, methodology, and periodic adjustments of the BRU shall be determined by the Combined Trusts Board and Steward President, maintained in the Private Annex, and subject to audit and disclosure requirements.

59.11.6 Activation Conditions

The Contingency Valuation Framework may be activated upon the occurrence of defined market stress conditions, including but not limited to:

- Sustained instability or loss of reliability in USD valuation

- Severe disruption in foreign exchange markets

- Significant divergence among major reserve assets

- Systemic monetary or liquidity crises affecting pricing mechanisms

Such conditions shall be:

- Based on objective and verifiable indicators, and

- Evaluated using transparent and auditable data sources

59.11.7 Governance and Authorization

Activation of the BRU framework shall follow a structured governance process:

- Detection of qualifying conditions through system monitoring

- Review and recommendation by the Combined Trusts Board

- Final authorization by the Steward President

Emergency provisions may allow expedited activation where necessary to preserve system integrity.

59.11.8 Continuity and Non-Devaluation

Transition to the Basket Reference Unit shall:

- Constitute a change in valuation reference only

- Not result in loss of value for Credit holders

- Not affect balances, contracts, or obligations

All Credits shall continue to be fully backed and over-collateralized in accordance with Article 59.4.

59.11.9 Transparency and Disclosure

Upon activation:

- The BRU composition and methodology shall be publicly disclosed

- Valuation updates shall be communicated clearly to all stakeholders

- Ongoing reporting shall ensure transparency and confidence

59.11.10 Reversion to Primary Reference

The system may revert to USD-based valuation upon stabilization of market conditions, subject to the same governance, verification, and transparency standards.

59.12 FX Rate Integrity, Settlement Architecture & System Boundaries

59.12.1 Purpose

This Article establishes the principles governing:

(a) Foreign exchange (FX) rate integrity

(b) Transaction execution and rate protection

(c) Blockchain-based settlement

(d) System boundaries, controls, and security

(e) Alignment with global financial systems

The objective is to ensure stability, transparency, and non-disruptive integration with existing monetary and FX frameworks.

59.12.2 Real-Time Market Alignment

(a) All currency conversions within the system shall be executed using real-time market exchange rates sourced from verified liquidity providers.

(b) The system shall not:

- Create synthetic exchange rates

- Fix or administratively determine currency values

- Operate parallel or alternative pricing systems

(c) All pricing shall remain fully aligned with:

- Licensed financial institutions

- FX liquidity providers

- National and international currency markets

59.12.3 Transaction Rate Lock Mechanism

(a) Upon initiation of any transaction, the applicable FX rate shall be immediately locked.

(b) The locked rate shall remain valid for a defined execution window, not exceeding sixty (60) seconds unless otherwise specified by system parameters.

(c) Once locked:

- The transaction shall be executed at the locked rate

- Subsequent market fluctuations shall not affect the transaction outcome

(d) Where a transaction is not completed within the validity window:

- The quote shall expire automatically

- A new rate shall be fetched

- User reconfirmation shall be required

 59.12.4 Handling of FX Rate Fluctuations

(a) FX rate changes shall apply only to newly initiated transactions.

(b) Transactions in progress shall remain protected under the rate lock mechanism.

(c) The system shall ensure that:

- Volatility is isolated from users during execution

- Pricing remains predictable and transparent

59.12.5 Internal FX Consistency and Integrity Controls

(a) The system shall operate a unified FX rate engine across all corridors.

(b) All conversion legs, including:

- Local Currency to Credit (Entry)

- Credit to Local Currency (Exit)

shall remain synchronized and consistent.

(c) The system shall prevent:

- Rate mismatches between transaction legs

- Timing-based arbitrage opportunities

- Financial losses to liquidity partners arising from inconsistencies

59.12.6 Blockchain Settlement Layer in Governance OS

(a) The system shall utilize blockchain infrastructure strictly as a settlement layer.

(b) Blockchain usage shall be limited to:

- Transaction finality

- Record integrity and immutability

- Reduction of counterparty risk

(c) The system shall not:

- Use blockchain for speculative pricing

- Derive exchange rates from blockchain markets

(d) All transactions shall achieve near-instant settlement finality, subject to network conditions.

59.12.7 System Boundaries and Operational Role

(a) The Foundation shall operate exclusively at the settlement and coordination layer.

(b) The Foundation shall not:

- Participate in live FX trading

- Act as a market maker

- Set or influence exchange rates

(c) All FX pricing and liquidity provision shall remain the responsibility of external licensed partners.

59.12.8 Wallet, Access and Closed-Loop Controls

(a) All system wallets shall be tied to verified on-ramp and off-ramp partners.

(b) The system shall operate as a closed-loop financial environment.

(c) The architecture shall prevent:

- Arbitrage through VPN or location spoofing

- Unauthorized access to regional pricing differences

- External bypass of the $200m limit FX under credits both in currency auto-conversion or wallet to wallet involving change in currency bases and regulatory controls

(d) Access and participation shall be subject to:

- Identity verification requirements

- Partner-level compliance enforcement

59.12.9 FX Market Compatibility

(a) The system shall operate within existing FX markets, not outside them. INcreasing currency circulation.

(b) Credit-Routed FX is designed to complement traditional FX by providing a faster, cheaper, and more accessible alternative for transactions under $200M.

(c) The system shall preserve the role of:

Major global currencies, including USD

Institutional liquidity providers

National monetary systems

(d) The system shall not interfere with:

Macro-level FX pricing

Central bank policy mechanisms

International financial stability

(e) It shall seek to enable faster, stable and cheaper transactions for the lower FX market where there is friction.

(f) Transactions exceeding $200M shall be routed through traditional FX markets and institutional liquidity channels.

59.12.10 Transaction Scope Limitation

(a) The system shall process transactions below a defined threshold of Two Hundred Million United States Dollars (USD 200,000,000) equivalent.

(b) Transactions exceeding this threshold shall be routed through:

- Traditional FX markets

- Institutional liquidity channels

(c) This limitation ensures:

- Preservation of macroeconomic stability

- Clear separation between operational layers

59.12.11 Economic and Systemic Impact

The architecture established under this Article shall:

(a) Accelerate intra-African currency movement

(b) Increase circulation of USD and major currencies within African markets

(c) Reduce friction in low-to-mid value cross-border transactions

(d) Enhance financial accessibility and efficiency

59.12.12 Principle of Non-Disruption

(a) The system shall function as an efficiency layer, not a replacement for existing financial systems.

(b) It shall:

- Reinforce global financial structures

- Maintain compatibility with international markets

- Avoid distortion of currency valuation mechanisms

59.13 Liquidity, Partner Roles & Spread Governance

59.13.1 Purpose

This Article establishes the framework governing:

(a) Liquidity provision within the system

(b) Roles and responsibilities of participating partners

(c) Spread application and margin distribution

(d) Risk allocation and sustainability of transaction flows

The objective is to ensure efficient liquidity access, fair partner participation, and stable system-wide economics.

59.13.2 Liquidity Provision Framework

(a) All transaction liquidity shall be provided by licensed and verified external partners, including:

- Financial institutions

- Payment service providers

- FX liquidity providers

(b) The system shall not act as a primary liquidity provider but shall:

- Coordinate access to partner liquidity

- Route transactions efficiently across available pools

(c) Liquidity shall be maintained across:

- Local currency corridors (e.g., NGN, KES, ZAR)

- Major global currencies (e.g., USD, EUR)

 59.13.3 Role of Liquidity Partners

(a) Liquidity partners shall be responsible for:

- Providing executable FX rates

- Supporting on-ramp and off-ramp conversions

- Absorbing short-term FX exposure during transaction execution

(b) Partners shall operate under:

- Predefined service-level agreements (SLAs)

- Compliance with regulatory and licensing requirements

(c) Partners shall not:

- Manipulate pricing outside market-aligned ranges

- Exploit timing or system inefficiencies

59.13.4 Spread and Margin Structure

(a) Each transaction shall include a spread component, consisting of:

- Government tax (0.25% to 0.30% for on/off-ramp, 0.10% to 0.30% for local transactions)

- Partner margin, determined by liquidity providers

(b) The total transaction cost shall remain:

- Competitive with or lower than traditional cross-border alternatives

- Transparent and disclosed prior to transaction confirmation

(c) The Foundation does not charge any transaction fees.

59.13.5 Spread Governance Principles

(a) Spread application shall follow these principles:

- Transparency - all costs visible to users before execution

- Fairness - equitable distribution between partners and government

- Market alignment - spreads reflect real liquidity conditions

(b) A maximum spread threshold may be defined to:

- Prevent excessive pricing

- Protect end users

- Maintain competitiveness

(c) Partner margins shall remain flexible within controlled boundaries to:

- Encourage participation

- Reflect corridor-specific liquidity conditions

59.13.6 Internal Pricing Consistency

(a) The system shall enforce uniform pricing logic across all transaction legs.

(b) Entry and exit conversions shall be:

- Synchronized

- Derived from the same rate source and timing

(c) This prevents:

- Internal arbitrage

- Pricing discrepancies across corridors

- Unintended financial losses

59.13.7 Risk Allocation

(a) Short-term FX exposure arising from rate locking shall be borne by:

- Liquidity partners

- Supported by spread margins and execution speed

(b) The system shall minimize risk exposure through:

- Near-instant transaction settlement

- Efficient routing and netting mechanisms

(c) Users shall not bear intra-transaction FX risk once a rate is locked.

59.13.8 Incentive Alignment

(a) The system shall ensure that:

- Liquidity partners are economically incentivized to participate

- Margins remain sufficient to sustain liquidity provision

(b) Incentives shall promote:

- High availability of liquidity

- Competitive pricing

- Reliable execution across corridors

59.13.9 Liquidity Discipline and Limits

(a) Liquidity usage shall remain within defined operational limits, including:

- Transaction size thresholds (as defined in Article 59.12)

- Corridor-specific capacity constraints

(b) The system shall avoid:

- Over-reliance on single liquidity sources

- Concentration risk

(c) Diversified liquidity sourcing shall be maintained at all times.

59.13.10 Netting and Flow Optimization

(a) The system may implement internal netting mechanisms to:

- Offset opposing transaction flows

- Reduce external FX demand

(b) Netting shall:

- Improve efficiency

- Lower liquidity costs

- Reduce exposure for partners

(c) All netting operations shall remain consistent with:

- Market-aligned pricing

- Transparency and auditability requirements

59.13.11 Economic Impact

The framework established under this Article shall:

(a) Enable sustainable liquidity across African and global corridors

(b) Strengthen participation of local financial institutions

(c) Reduce transaction costs through competitive spreads

(d) Support scalable growth of cross-border financial activity

59.13.12 Interpretation

This Article shall be interpreted to ensure:

- Sustainable liquidity provision

- Fair and transparent pricing

- Protection of all ecosystem participants

- Long-term stability of the system

59.14 Core Article Status

This Article shall be interpreted to uphold:

- Transaction integrity

- Market alignment

- Partner protection

- System security

- Global financial compatibility

This Article LIX is designated as a Tier 1 Core Article under Article XIX.3.

No amendment may:

(a) Remove the asset-backing requirement, or change the transaction percentages for more profit;

(b) Introduce interest, yield, dividends, or speculative features;

(c) Alter the bridge-only, non-competing nature of Jowih Credits;

(d) Remove government revenue share on on-ramp and off-ramp conversions;

(e) Convert Jowih Credits into a security or investment vehicle;

(f) Eliminate IAG audit requirements;

(g) Permit listing on external speculative cryptocurrency exchanges;

(h) Reduce the reserve backing ratio below 100%;

(i) Force Credits for domestic transactions;

(j) Redesign Jowih Wallet in a way to do away with PSPs or banks as the default face;

(k) Allow partners as executioner to hold Credits as an asset rather than acting solely as conversion rails;

(l) Allow Credits to enter the domestic economy as a currency - Credits must always convert to local currency for domestic spending;

(m) Change Auto-Conversion from the default mode;

(n) Make Holding Credits mandatory - Holding must remain optional;

(o) Operate any fiat on-ramp or off-ramp service - the Foundation shall not operate any conversion service;

(p) Change the positioning of Jowih Credits from a complementary bridge that stimulates FX activity to a system that seeks to replace or compete with traditional FX markets;

(q) Pin credits backing to a single currency;

(r) Remove the need for BRU to value credits during crisis in the market;

(s) Remove the governance or triggers set herein.

59.15 Severability

If any provision of this Article is held invalid, the remaining provisions shall remain in full force and effect. The Foundation shall seek to achieve the purpose of this Article through all lawful means.

ARTICLE LX - OPERATIONAL FRAMEWORK AND PRIVATE ANNEX

Recital

The Constitution establishes the foundational principles, governance, and limits for Jowih Credits. The detailed implementation of these principles requires a comprehensive Operational Framework that sets forth the specific mechanics, rates, procedures, and technical standards necessary for the day-to-day operation of Jowih Credits.

Jowih Credits operates through a partner-only execution model. The Foundation issues and holds the credit. Partners handle ALL conversions. The Foundation does not operate any exchange, conversion, or execution service.

The Foundation does not earn from transaction fees. Its primary economic model is the Treasury Engine - holding Credits and investing the float generated by held Credits in mission-aligned projects, with all returns flowing to the Mission Fund.

The Operational Framework shall ensure that Credits remain a bridge layer only, never entering the domestic economy. Auto-Conversion shall be the default mode for all transactions. Holding Credits shall be optional.

Certain operational details, including commercial terms with partners, pricing arrangements, and sensitive technical specifications, shall be maintained in a Private Annex to protect commercial confidentiality and competitive positioning.

60.1 Establishment

(a) The Combined Trusts Board shall adopt, maintain, and publish a Jowih Credits Operational Framework (hereinafter "the Operational Framework") that implements the provisions of Article LIX of this Constitution.

(b) The Combined Trusts Board shall also adopt and maintain a Private Annex to the Operational Framework (hereinafter "Private Annex") containing confidential operational details not suitable for public disclosure.

(c) The Operational Framework shall be a public document, published on the Public Dashboard (Article XXXV), and accessible to all users, partners, governments, and regulators.

(d) The Private Annex shall be restricted to the Combined Trusts Board, the Steward President, the IAG Departments, and such other persons as the Combined Trusts Board may designate.

60.2 Content

(a) Operational Framework (Public)

The Operational Framework shall include, but shall not be limited to:

(i) Fee schedules and government tax remittance mechanisms;

(ii) Partner integration standards, technical specifications, and onboarding procedures;

(iii) Reserve management procedures, liquidity protocols, and settlement guarantee operations;

(iv) User operational procedures, including wallet operations, conversions, and holding period enforcement;

(v) Reporting schedules, transparency requirements, and audit protocols;

(vi) Partner margin guidelines and competitive frameworks;

(vii) Auto-Conversion default settings and user preferences;

(viii) Procedures ensuring Credits never enter the domestic economy.

(b) Private Annex (Confidential)

The Private Annex may include, but shall not be limited to:

(i) Specific commercial terms with individual PSPS, bank, card network, and fintech partners;

(ii) Confidential pricing arrangements and revenue sharing percentages;

(iii) Proprietary technical specifications and security protocols;

(iv) Reserve composition details that could affect market stability if disclosed;

(v) BRU composition and methodology details;

(vi) Such other confidential matters as the Combined Trusts Board may designate.

 60.3 Adoption and Amendment

(a) Operational Framework (Public)

(i) The Combined Trusts Board shall adopt the initial Operational Framework within one hundred and eighty (180) days of the effective date of this Constitution.

(ii) Amendments to the Operational Framework shall be subject to a two-thirds (⅔) supermajority vote of the Combined Trusts Board.

(iii) Any amendment to the Operational Framework that would conflict with Article LIX or any other provision of this Constitution shall be null and void and subject to mission threatening action.

(b) Private Annex (Confidential)

(i) The Combined Trusts Board shall adopt the initial Private Annex within one hundred and eighty (180) days of the effective date of this Constitution.

(ii) Amendments to the Private Annex, including any part thereof, shall require a two-thirds (⅔) supermajority vote of the Combined Trusts Board.

(iii) Any amendment to the Private Annex that would conflict with Article LIX, the Operational Framework, or any other provision of this Constitution shall be null and void and subject to mission threatening action.

60.4 Relationship to the Constitution

(a) The Operational Framework and Private Annex are subordinate to this Constitution. In the event of any conflict, the Constitution shall prevail.

(b) The Operational Framework and Private Annex shall be interpreted in a manner consistent with the purposes, principles, and provisions of this Constitution.

(c) Nothing in the Operational Framework or Private Annex shall be construed to:

(i) Grant any ownership, voting, profit, interest, yield, or investment rights in the Foundation;

(ii) Permit the speculative trading of Jowih Credits;

(iii) Authorize the Foundation to compete with PSPSs, banks, card networks, or fintech platforms;

(iv) Undermine the non-speculative nature of Jowih Credits as set forth in Article LIX;

(v) Allow Credits to enter the domestic economy as a currency;

(vi) Change Auto-Conversion from the default mode;

(vii) Make Holding Credits mandatory;

(viii) Authorize the Foundation to operate any conversion service - the Foundation shall not operate any fiat on-ramp or off-ramp service.

60.5 Review and Audit

(a) The Combined Trusts Board through the company responsible for Jowih Credits shall review the Operational Framework and Private Annex annually and may adopt amendments in accordance with Article 60.3.

(b) The IAG Departments shall audit compliance with the Operational Framework and Private Annex as part of their regular audit functions.

(c) Audit findings relating to the Private Annex shall be reported to the Combined Trusts Board and Steward President and shall remain confidential.

60.6 Confidentiality

(a) The Private Annex and its contents shall be treated as confidential and shall not be disclosed except as required by law or as authorized by the Combined Trusts Board.

(b) Any person granted access to the Private Annex shall be bound by a confidentiality obligation.

(c) The Combined Trusts Board may declassify portions of the Private Annex and move them to the public Operational Framework if it determines that confidentiality is no longer required.

60.7 Core Article Status

This Article is designated as a Tier 1 Core Article under Article XIX.3 and may be amended only through the procedures therein.
